title:	Atomic ref updates within a sync run (git push --mirror parity)
state:	OPEN
author:	Soph (Stefan Haubold)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	46
--
## Problem

`git push --mirror` to an atomic-capable receive-pack is all-or-nothing within one push: if any ref command fails, none of them apply. git-sync's pipeline is multi-phase, so a failure mid-pipeline leaves the target in a partial state:

- **Bootstrap (one-shot)**: branches and tags share one push, so this path is already effectively atomic if the server supports it.
- **Bootstrap (batched)**: branch batches push first (each batch is its own receive-pack POST), then a tail phase pushes tags + other-kind refs. A failure in the tail leaves the branches landed, tags missing.
- **Sync incremental relay**: one push, atomic-capable.
- **Sync materialized fallback**: one push.
- **Sync `--prune`**: deletes piggyback on the same push as updates, so within sync this is one transaction.
- **Replicate**: relay push for create/update, then a separate `PushCommands` for deletes ([internal/strategy/replicate/replicate.go:78](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/blob/main/internal/strategy/replicate/replicate.go#L78)). Two transactions; a failure between them leaves stale refs un-pruned.

The receive-pack `atomic` capability (per `Documentation/gitprotocol-pack.adoc`) lets the client signal that all commands in one update-request must apply or none. We don't currently set it on any push.

## Sketch

1. Add an `atomic` capability check to `gitproto.TargetFeaturesFromAdvRefs`.
2. When negotiated, set the capability on `packp.UpdateRequests` in `buildUpdateRequest`.
3. Where the multi-phase shape is the actual non-atomicity (batched-bootstrap tail, replicate-then-delete), the capability alone won't fix it — we'd need to either fold deletes into the same push as updates (replicate) or accept that batched bootstrap is by design multi-transaction. The capability still helps within each phase.

## BestEffort interaction

`BestEffort` downgrades per-ref ng statuses to warnings. With `atomic`, a single ng causes the whole update to fail server-side — so atomic + BestEffort would mutually defeat each other in the obvious way. Sensible default: skip atomic when BestEffort is set, or fail loudly if both are explicitly requested.
```

> AGENT

I've read through issue #46. Quick summary of how I'm reading it:

- The core ask is to use receive-pack's `atomic` capability so a single push is all-or-nothing.
- The honest finding in the issue itself: `atomic` only helps *within* one push. The real non-atomicity comes from the multi-phase shapes — batched bootstrap (branches → tail) and replicate (relay push → separate delete `PushCommands`).
- The two phase-shape issues each have a different answer: replicate can fold deletes into the same push, but batched bootstrap is multi-transaction by design.
- And `atomic` + `BestEffort` cancel each other out — they need an explicit interaction rule.

A few things I'd want to chat about before touching code:

1. **Scope.** Is the goal here just "negotiate and set `atomic` where pushes are already one-shot" (cheap, partial win), or also "restructure replicate to one push" (more invasive, actually closes the gap)? They're separable.
2. **BestEffort policy.** Your sketch suggests "skip atomic when BestEffort is set." That seems right to me — silent skip with a debug log, error only if a user explicitly asks for both. Agree?
3. **Batched bootstrap.** Are you okay leaving that as documented-multi-transaction, or is there appetite to make the tail push optional / fold it into the last batch when small?

What's your priority on this — quick win first, or do you want to design the replicate change too?

title:	Surface force-with-lease semantics; consider --force-blind opt-out
state:	OPEN
author:	Soph (Stefan Haubold)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	47
--
## Background

`git push --force-with-lease[=<refname>:<expect>]` refuses a force-update if the remote tip doesn't match what the client thinks it currently is. Protects against clobbering concurrent updates from another writer.

git-sync runs are stateless across runs, so there's no equivalent to git's remote-tracking refs — but **within** a run the session captures target ref state at the start (`s.target.refMap`), and that's effectively the lease.

## What we already do

`convert.PlansToPushCommands` ([internal/convert/convert.go](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/blob/main/internal/convert/convert.go)) sets:

```go
PushCommand{
    Name: p.TargetRef,
    Old:  p.TargetHash,   // captured at session start
    New:  p.SourceHash,
    ...
}
```

Receive-pack rejects a command whose `Old` doesn't match the ref's current value on target ("stale info" / "remote ref does not match expected old value"). So **the protocol-level race check is already happening on every update**. If a target ref moves between when we read it and when we push, the update is rejected — that's the lease behavior, just not framed as such anywhere.

## What's missing

1. **Documentation.** Users running git-sync periodically against a target with concurrent writers should know that --force is already safe-by-default in this sense, not unconditional clobber.
2. **Opt-out.** A user mirroring from an authoritative source into a write-locked target may want to bypass the lease on purpose (e.g., recovering a target that was manually edited mid-mirror). No way to do that today. Possible flag: `--force-blind` that sends `Old: zeroHash` for force updates, telling receive-pack "don't care what's there."
3. **Reason text.** When a lease check fails, the user sees the receive-pack ng reason. Worth adding a planner-side hint: "target ref moved during sync; rerun, or use --force-blind to overwrite."

## Stateless angle

The natural lease window is one sync run. There's no concept of "what target looked like last time we ran" — that would require persistent state, which git-sync deliberately avoids. Per-run lease is the right scope: it protects against races within the sync window (other writers touching target while we're fetching from source) without requiring git-sync to remember anything between runs.
```

> AGENT

Got it — that reframes #46 nicely. Reading the two together:

- **#47 is the safety net.** Every `PushCommand` already carries `Old: TargetHash` captured at session start, so receive-pack rejects any update where the target moved underneath us. That's the lease.
- **#46 atomic is for "this single push is naturally all-or-nothing"**, not "make the whole sync run atomic."

So the scope you're describing is:

1. Negotiate `atomic` and set it on the pushes that are already one transaction: one-shot bootstrap, sync relay, materialized fallback, sync+prune.
2. For batched bootstrap: set atomic on **each batch** individually. We assume no concurrent target changes for the initial flow; if that assumption breaks, the next batch's lease check (#47) catches it because its `Old` won't match anymore.
3. Don't restructure replicate's two-push shape. Atomic still applies to the relay push; the delete push remains its own transaction.
4. `BestEffort` + atomic: skip atomic when BestEffort is set; error only if a user explicitly passes both.

Two things to nail down before I touch code:

- **Default on or opt-in?** My read: on by default when the server advertises the capability — it's strictly safer and matches `git push --mirror` parity. Any reason to gate it behind a flag?
- **Per-batch atomic in batched bootstrap** — confirming you want this. It adds nothing for the cross-batch story, but it does mean a single bad ref in batch N doesn't half-land that batch.

Sound right?

> AGENT

Good question — that's the exact tension. Here's how I think it shakes out:

**Pull refs today** are handled one of two ways:
1. `--exclude-ref-prefix refs/pull/` — they never enter the push at all.
2. `--all-refs` which bundles `BestEffort` — they hit the push, server returns `ng`, BestEffort downgrades each one to a warning.

**Atomic conflicts only with (2).** If BestEffort is on, even one `ng` from a pull ref under atomic fails the whole transaction → no refs land → the user gets nothing instead of "most things plus warnings." That defeats the BestEffort UX.

**Resolution:** atomic and BestEffort are mutually exclusive. Concretely:

- BestEffort on → never set atomic, regardless of server capability. Per-ref `ng` continues to come back individually, warn-skip works.
- BestEffort off → atomic safe to negotiate. If a pull ref (or anything else) gets `ng`, the user already wants the whole sync to fail; atomic just makes it fail cleanly with nothing partially landed.

So pull refs don't need any new handling — they ride on the existing BestEffort/exclude paths. The atomic feature only kicks in on the "strict" code path where rejection-fails-the-sync is already the contract.

One edge I want to flag: `--all-refs` on sync/plan implies BestEffort, which means atomic is effectively *off* for the most common GitHub-mirror configuration. That's arguably fine (correctness > parity), but it does mean the `git push --mirror` parity claim is narrower than it sounds — atomic mainly helps non-`--all-refs` runs and `replicate` (which doesn't bundle BestEffort with `--all-refs`).

Want me to write that scope clarification into the issue, or leave it and just implement?

> DEVELOPER

but from what we looked git push mirror did also only warn about github special refs and not fail? or does it?

> AGENT

You're right to push on this — I was sloppy and so is the issue's framing.

`git push --mirror` by itself is **not** atomic. It's `git push --mirror --atomic` that's all-or-nothing. Default `--mirror` behavior:

- Sends all ref updates in one push.
- Each ref gets its own `ok`/`ng` from the server.
- GitHub's `refs/pull/*` come back `ng`, the rest land.
- `git` prints `! [remote rejected] refs/pull/1/head (deny updating a hidden ref)` per failing ref.
- Exit code is non-zero, but partial application on the target is exactly what happens.

So "git push --mirror warns about hidden refs and the rest go through" matches reality for the *non-atomic* form. That's not the parity #46 is going for — the issue is really after `git push --mirror --atomic` parity, which is opt-in on the git side too.

This actually argues for a different default than I suggested earlier:

- **Don't enable atomic by default** even when the server advertises it. Default git-sync behavior should match default `git push --mirror`: per-ref `ng` flows back, BestEffort can downgrade.
- **Add a flag** (`--atomic`?) that opts in, same as git. Then atomic + BestEffort being incompatible is the user's explicit choice, not something we hide.

That also fixes the awkward case I flagged at the end of my last message — `--all-refs` no longer silently disables atomic, because atomic was never on unless asked.

Does that match how you think about it? If so, the issue title and "Sketch" section want a small revision before I touch code.

> DEVELOPER

Agreed on both counts. Let me draft a rewrite — want to confirm before I edit the issue since it's public.

Proposed new body:

---

## Status

git-sync doesn't negotiate or set the receive-pack `atomic` capability today. Logging this so we don't lose track of the option.

## Background

The `atomic` capability ([gitprotocol-pack.adoc](https://git-scm.com/docs/gitprotocol-pack)) lets a client signal that all ref commands in one update-request apply or none do. It's what `git push --atomic` sets — **not** what `git push --mirror` sets. Default `--mirror` is per-ref: each command gets its own `ok`/`ng`, partial application is normal.

Where git-sync stands relative to `git push`:
- Default `git push --mirror` → per-ref outcomes, partial apply. git-sync today matches this.
- `git push --mirror --atomic` → all-or-nothing within one push. git-sync has no equivalent.

## Why we haven't built it

1. **No demand yet.** No one has asked for transactional pushes; the per-ref model has been fine.
2. **Conflicts with `BestEffort`.** `--all-refs` on sync/plan bundles BestEffort, which downgrades per-ref `ng` to warnings — atomic would defeat that, so atomic would have to be off for the most common GitHub-mirror config anyway.
3. **Phase shape, not capability, is the real gap.** Where git-sync is non-atomic across phases (batched bootstrap tail, replicate's separate delete push), the `atomic` capability alone doesn't help — those are multi-transaction by design.

## If we revisit

- Add an explicit `--atomic` flag (opt-in, matching git's UX).
- Negotiate via `gitproto.TargetFeaturesFromAdvRefs`, set on `packp.UpdateRequests` in `buildUpdateRequest`.
- Mutually exclusive with `BestEffort`: error if both are set explicitly.
- Per-batch only in batched bootstrap; the cross-batch story stays the lease check from #47.

---

Two things to decide:

- Keep the issue **open** as "logged, not planned" or **close** it with this as the rationale comment?
- Strip the implementation sketch entirely, or leave the "If we revisit" section as a hint for future-us?

It's what `git push --atomic` sets — **not** what `git push --mirror` sets. Default `--mirror` is per-ref: each command gets its own `ok`/`ng`, partial application is normal.\n\nWhere git-sync stands relative to `git push`:\n- Default `git push --mirror` → per-ref outcomes, partial apply. git-sync today matches this.\n- `git push --mirror --atomic` → all-or-nothing within one push. git-sync has no equivalent.\n\n## Why we haven't built it\n\n1. **No demand yet.** No one has asked for transactional pushes; the per-ref model has been fine.\n2. **Conflicts with `BestEffort`.** `--all-refs` on sync/plan bundles `BestEffort`, which downgrades per-ref `ng` to warnings — atomic would defeat that, so it would have to be off for the most common GitHub-mirror config anyway.\n3. **Phase shape, not capability, is the real gap.** Where git-sync is non-atomic across phases (batched bootstrap tail, replicate's separate delete push), the `atomic` capability alone doesn't help — those are multi-transaction by design.\n\nRelated: #47 (force-with-lease semantics — the per-run lease is what protects against concurrent target writes today, including across batches in bootstrap).\nEOF\n)\"",

1	// Package convert provides shared type conversions between planner and gitproto
2	// types. It exists to avoid duplicating these helpers across strategy packages,
3	// while keeping planner and gitproto free of circular imports.
4	package convert
5	
6	import (
7		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing"
8	
9		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/gitproto"
10		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/planner"
11	)
12	
13	// DesiredRefs converts planner desired refs to gitproto desired refs.
14	func DesiredRefs(desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef) map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef {
15		out := make(map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef, len(desired))
16		for k, v := range desired {
17			out[k] = gitproto.DesiredRef{
18				SourceRef:  v.SourceRef,
19				TargetRef:  v.TargetRef,
20				SourceHash: v.SourceHash,
21				IsTag:      v.Kind == planner.RefKindTag,
22			}
23		}
24		return out
25	}
26	
27	// DesiredRefsForPlans converts only the desired refs referenced by the plans.
28	func DesiredRefsForPlans(
29		desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef,
30		plans []planner.BranchPlan,
31	) map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef {
32		out := make(map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef, len(plans))
33		for _, plan := range plans {
34			v, ok := desired[plan.TargetRef]
35			if !ok {
36				continue
37			}
38			out[plan.TargetRef] = gitproto.DesiredRef{
39				SourceRef:  v.SourceRef,
40				TargetRef:  v.TargetRef,
41				SourceHash: v.SourceHash,
42				IsTag:      v.Kind == planner.RefKindTag,
43			}
44		}
45		return out
46	}
47	
48	// PlansToPushPlans converts planner BranchPlans to gitproto PushPlans.
49	func PlansToPushPlans(plans []planner.BranchPlan) []gitproto.PushPlan {
50		out := make([]gitproto.PushPlan, len(plans))
51		for i, p := range plans {
52			out[i] = gitproto.PushPlan{
53				TargetRef:  p.TargetRef,
54				TargetHash: p.TargetHash,
55				SourceHash: p.SourceHash,
56				Delete:     p.Action == planner.ActionDelete,
57			}
58		}
59		return out
60	}
61	
62	// PlansToPushCommands converts planner BranchPlans directly to gitproto PushCommands.
63	func PlansToPushCommands(plans []planner.BranchPlan) []gitproto.PushCommand {
64		out := make([]gitproto.PushCommand, len(plans))
65		for i, p := range plans {
66			out[i] = gitproto.PushCommand{
67				Name:   p.TargetRef,
68				Old:    p.TargetHash,
69				New:    p.SourceHash,
70				Delete: p.Action == planner.ActionDelete,
71			}
72			if out[i].Delete {
73				out[i].New = plumbing.ZeroHash
74			}
75		}
76		return out
77	}
78	
```

1	package gitproto
2	
3	import (
4		"bytes"
5		"context"
6		"errors"
7		"fmt"
8		"io"
9		"os"
10	
11		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing"
12		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/format/packfile"
13		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/protocol/packp"
14		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/protocol/packp/capability"
15		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/protocol/packp/sideband"
16		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/storer"
17		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/transport"
18	)
19	
20	// PushCommand represents a single ref update command.
21	type PushCommand struct {
22		Name   plumbing.ReferenceName
23		Old    plumbing.Hash
24		New    plumbing.Hash
25		Delete bool
26	}
27	
28	// Pusher wraps target-side receive-pack state behind a smaller execution API.
29	// When OnRejection is non-nil, per-ref ng statuses invoke it instead of erroring;
30	// pack-level unpack failure remains fatal.
31	//
32	// Returned by NewPusher as a pointer so callers can attach OnRejection after
33	// construction without worrying about whether downstream strategies have
34	// already captured a value copy.
35	type Pusher struct {
36		Conn        *Conn
37		Adv         *packp.AdvRefs
38		Verbose     bool
39		OnRejection func(refName plumbing.ReferenceName, status string)
40	}
41	
42	// NewPusher builds a target-side push executor.
43	func NewPusher(conn *Conn, adv *packp.AdvRefs, verbose bool) *Pusher {
44		return &Pusher{Conn: conn, Adv: adv, Verbose: verbose}
45	}
46	
47	// PushPack streams a pack to the target.
48	func (p *Pusher) PushPack(ctx context.Context, commands []PushCommand, pack io.ReadCloser) error {
49		return PushPack(ctx, p.Conn, p.Adv, commands, pack, p.Verbose, p.OnRejection)
50	}
51	
52	// PushCommands sends ref-only updates without a pack.
53	func (p *Pusher) PushCommands(ctx context.Context, commands []PushCommand) error {
54		return PushCommands(ctx, p.Conn, p.Adv, commands, p.Verbose, p.OnRejection)
55	}
56	
57	// PushObjects encodes and pushes locally materialized objects.
58	func (p *Pusher) PushObjects(ctx context.Context, commands []PushCommand, store storer.Storer, hashes []plumbing.Hash) error {
59		return PushObjects(ctx, p.Conn, p.Adv, commands, store, hashes, p.Verbose, p.OnRejection)
60	}
61	
62	// buildUpdateRequest builds the receive-pack update request.
63	func buildUpdateRequest(
64		adv *packp.AdvRefs,
65		commands []PushCommand,
66		verbose bool,
67	) (*packp.UpdateRequests, bool, bool, error) {
68		req := packp.NewUpdateRequests()
69		if sb := PreferredSideband(adv.Capabilities); sb != "" {
70			if err := req.Capabilities.Set(sb); err != nil {
71				return nil, false, false, fmt.Errorf("set capability: %w", err)
72			}
73		}
74		if adv.Capabilities.Supports(capability.ReportStatus) {
75			if err := req.Capabilities.Set(capability.ReportStatus); err != nil {
76				return nil, false, false, fmt.Errorf("set capability: %w", err)
77			}
78		}
79	
80		hasDelete := false
81		hasUpdates := false
82		for _, cmd := range commands {
83			c := &packp.Command{Name: cmd.Name, Old: cmd.Old}
84			if cmd.Delete {
85				c.New = plumbing.ZeroHash
86				hasDelete = true
87			} else {
88				c.New = cmd.New
89				hasUpdates = true
90			}
91			req.Commands = append(req.Commands, c)
92		}
93	
94		if hasDelete {
95			if !adv.Capabilities.Supports(capability.DeleteRefs) {
96				return nil, false, false, errors.New("target does not support delete-refs")
97			}
98			if err := req.Capabilities.Set(capability.DeleteRefs); err != nil {
99				return nil, false, false, fmt.Errorf("set capability: %w", err)
100			}
101		}
102	
103		_ = verbose // progress handling is server-side in HTTP mode
104		return req, hasDelete, hasUpdates, nil
105	}
106	
107	// sendReceivePack encodes and POSTs a receive-pack request, then decodes the report.
108	func sendReceivePack(
109		ctx context.Context,
110		conn *Conn,
111		req *packp.UpdateRequests,
112		packData io.Reader,
113		verbose bool,
114		onRejection func(plumbing.ReferenceName, string),
115	) error {
116		var header bytes.Buffer
117		if err := req.Encode(&header); err != nil {
118			return fmt.Errorf("encode update-request: %w", err)
119		}
120		body := io.Reader(bytes.NewReader(header.Bytes()))
```

> AGENT

Before I touch code, here's what I've found and how I'd scope it.

**Current state, verified:**
- `convert.PlansToPushCommands` ([internal/convert/convert.go:62-77](internal/convert/convert.go)) sets `Old: p.TargetHash` from the session-captured `s.target.refMap`. That's the lease.
- `cfg.Force` already exists, but only for `sync` — `bootstrap` and `replicate` reject `--force` explicitly ([syncer/syncer.go:930, :563](internal/syncer/syncer.go)).
- The server's `ng` reason text flows through `onRejection(name, status)` or wraps via `report.Error()` ([gitproto/push.go:149-163](internal/gitproto/push.go)). No git-sync-side interpretation today.

**Issue #47 has three sub-items with very different sizes:**

1. **Docs** — note in `docs/usage.md` that `--force` is already lease-safe within a run (target tip captured at session start). Cheap, no code change.
2. **Reason hint on lease failure** — wrap/augment the `ng` reason when it matches the "stale info / does not match expected old value" pattern. Low-risk; lives in the rejection-handling path.
3. **`--force-blind` flag** — new config, requires `--force`, only meaningful for sync, plumbed into convert so non-FF updates send `Old: ZeroHash`. Most invasive; needs tests across relay/materialized/batched paths.

**My recommendation:** ship 1 and 2 now; defer 3 with the same "comment if you need it" pattern we just applied to #46. Reasoning: the lease behavior is already correct and safe; the actual user-facing gap is just discoverability. `--force-blind` is a recovery escape hatch nobody has asked for yet, and it adds non-trivial surface.

Want me to:
- **(a)** Do 1+2 now, edit #47 to note 3 is deferred pending demand, or
- **(b)** Do all three, or
- **(c)** Just 1 (docs) and leave 2 and 3 as logged?

> DEVELOPER

so the force that exists is force with lease?

> AGENT

Yes — and that's exactly what #47 is calling out. The naming is misleading.

What `--force` actually does today:

1. **Planner** allows non-fast-forward updates and tag retargets (without `--force`, those would be blocked with a "use --force" reason) — `planner.go:325-355`.
2. **Push command** still sends `Old: TargetHash` from the session-captured `refMap` via `convert.PlansToPushCommands` — never zero. There's no code path that sends `Old: ZeroHash` for force updates.
3. **Receive-pack** rejects the command if the target's current value doesn't match `Old`. That's the lease.

So `--force` semantically is `git push --force-with-lease`, where the lease window is "this sync run." The flag controls *planner permissiveness* (allow non-FF), not *protocol-level clobber* (bypass the old-hash check). It's never raw `--force`.

That's why #47 proposes `--force-blind` as the real `git push --force` equivalent — the actual zero-old, don't-care-what's-there override. It doesn't exist today.

Given this, the docs item in #47 is more important than I framed it — users reading `--force` will assume git's default semantics and be surprised when their concurrent-writer scenario "works correctly." It's not a bug, but the name oversells the danger.

Want me to also consider **renaming or aliasing** `--force` → `--force-with-lease` (with `--force` kept as a compatibility alias)? Could be part of the docs pass, or a separate decision.

> DEVELOPER

I'd go with renaming --force to --force-with-lease and just add a real normal --force

# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).

## [0.4.3] - 2026-05-07

### Added

- `--bootstrap-strategy=topo` for merge-heavy repos: walks every reachable commit in deterministic topological order so batched bootstrap can place sub-pack boundaries inside side-branch ancestry ([#41](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/41))
- `--progress` for live per-side throughput across `sync`, `replicate`, `bootstrap`, and `fetch`, with rolling-window rate, hostname-aware labels, inline pack subdivision, and an end-of-run summary under `--stats` ([#37](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/37))
- Per-subcommand `--help` after the CLI moved to cobra; bare `git-sync` lists subcommands on stdout instead of printing the full usage block as an error ([#35](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/35))
- README cover image and embedded demo videos for `git-sync plan` and `git-sync sync` ([#30](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/30), [#31](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/31))

### Changed

- Batched bootstrap stream-parses the pack and aborts doomed pushes ~5% in instead of waiting for the body-limit rejection; subdivision converges in 1–2 rounds instead of 6+ on blob-heavy repos ([#40](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/40))
- Post-rejection subdivision sizes splits from observed pack bytes (4× when the server cut us off mid-stream, 2× otherwise) and ratchets the bytes-per-object estimate up after each 413 ([#38](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/38))
- Batched bootstrap recombines upcoming checkpoints when consecutive packs underuse the target limit, recovering pack granularity after heavy regions; already-pushed checkpoints are also passed as fetch `have`s on later rounds ([#42](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/42))
- Relay-only syncs skip the upfront `FetchToStore`; the materialized fallback lazy-fetches the source closure only when force, prune, or divergent refs require it ([#34](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/34))

### Fixed

- Sync into targets that share reachability with the source: tolerate pruned objects in the materialized walker, accept branch creates and `no-thin` targets in incremental relay, and surface diagnostic headers (`Cf-Ray`, `Server`, `X-Request-Id`) on opaque 5xx ([#33](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/33))

## [0.4.2] - 2026-04-30

First public release. `git-sync` mirrors refs from a source remote to a target
remote without a local checkout, streaming source packs directly into target
`receive-pack` whenever possible. The release covers the CLI, the library API,
and the protocol plumbing they share.

### Added

- `git-sync sync` — relay-based mirror that streams source `upload-pack`
  output into target `receive-pack` without materializing the object graph
  locally. Falls back to an in-memory `go-git` store, bounded by
  `--materialized-max-objects`, when relay is not eligible (force, prune,
  deletes, tag retargets) ([#1](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/1),
  [#2](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/2)).
- `git-sync replicate` and `git-sync plan --mode replicate` for
  source-authoritative, relay-only replication. Divergent branches and tags
  are retargeted against the source; `--prune` deletes orphan managed refs.
  Relay-only by design: no materialized fallback
  ([#4](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/4)).
- `git-sync plan` — preview the actions a `sync` or `replicate` would take,
  with structured JSON output suitable for automation.
- `git-sync bootstrap` — initial-seed path for empty targets, with adaptive
```

> AGENT

Before I start typing — three decisions where I want a quick call:

1. **Migration approach.** Project is pre-1.0 (0.4.3), so hard breaking changes are tolerable. Options:
   - **(a) Hard switch**: `--force` immediately means blind; old `--force` users get blind semantics silently. Risky if anyone scripted it.
   - **(b) Deprecation alias**: `--force` still works for one release but prints a warning; require `--force-with-lease` or new `--force-blind` (then flip in next release). Safer but adds a transitional name.
   - **(c) Removal**: `--force` errors out with "use --force-with-lease or --force-blind"; no silent behavior change. Forces users to read the change.

   My pick: **(c)** — pre-1.0, no installed base of scripts we know of, and silent semantics change is the worst outcome. CHANGELOG entry + clear error message.

2. **Both flags at once.** `--force --force-with-lease` should error out (incompatible) — yes?

3. **Blind = zero all olds, or only forced refs?** Real `git push --force` zeros `Old` for every ref in the push (not just the non-FF ones). Matching that is simpler and matches user mental model — agreed?

If you're good with (c), yes, yes — I'll start with task #1.

> DEVELOPER

I do 1

> AGENT

To make sure — by "1" do you mean migration option **(a) hard switch** (silent: `--force` immediately means blind, old scripts get new semantics with no warning), or did you mean "start with task 1"?

The difference matters: (a) is silent breaking; (c) is loud breaking (errors out, forces users to pick). Pre-1.0 either is defensible, but I want to be sure.

> DEVELOPER

Going with hard switch then. `--force` flips to blind semantics, `--force-with-lease` carries the old behavior. Starting on task #1.

25	func newSyncLikeCmd(name, short string, dryRun bool, defaultMode gitsync.OperationMode) *cobra.Command {
26		var (
27			mappings    []string
28			jsonOutput  bool
29			sourceAuth  gitsync.EndpointAuth
30			targetAuth  gitsync.EndpointAuth
31			branches    string

39			Short:         short,
40			Args:          cobra.MaximumNArgs(2),
41			SilenceErrors: true,
42			SilenceUsage:  true,
43			RunE: func(cmd *cobra.Command, args []string) error {
44				req.Policy.Mode = gitsync.OperationMode(modeValue)
45				req.Policy.Protocol = gitsync.ProtocolMode(protocolVal)
46	
47				if req.Source.URL == "" && len(args) > 0 {
48					req.Source.URL = args[0]
49				}
50				if req.Target.URL == "" && len(args) > 1 {
51					req.Target.URL = args[1]
52				}
53	
54				if branches != "" {
55					req.Scope.Branches = splitCSV(branches)
56				}
57				for _, raw := range mappings {
58					mapping, err := validation.ParseMapping(raw)
59					if err != nil {
60						return fmt.Errorf("parse mapping %q: %w", raw, err)
61					}
62					req.Scope.Mappings = append(req.Scope.Mappings, gitsync.RefMapping{
63						Source: mapping.Source,
64						Target: mapping.Target,
65					})
66				}
67	
68				if req.Source.URL == "" || req.Target.URL == "" {
69					return fmt.Errorf("%s requires source and target repository URLs", name)
70				}
71	
72				client := unstable.New(unstable.Options{
73					Auth: gitsync.StaticAuthProvider{Source: sourceAuth, Target: targetAuth},
74				})
75	
76				var (
77					result unstable.Result
78					err    error
79				)
80				ctx := cmd.Context()
81				switch {
82				case dryRun:
83					result, err = client.Plan(ctx, req)
84				case req.Policy.Mode == gitsync.ModeReplicate:
85					result, err = client.Replicate(ctx, req)
86				default:
87					result, err = client.Sync(ctx, req)
88				}
89				if err != nil {
90					return fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", name, err)
91				}
92				printOutput(jsonOutput, result)
93	
94				if !dryRun && result.Blocked > 0 {
95					return errors.New("one or more branches were skipped because the target was not fast-forwardable")
96				}
97				return nil
98			},
99		}
100

102		addTargetEndpoint(cmd, &req.Target)

204	// --map remappings push the correct (mapped) target ref first.

1	// Package incremental implements the incremental relay strategy for git-sync.
2	// This fast-path streams a pack from source directly to target when all updates
3	// are fast-forward branch updates or new tag creates.
4	package incremental
5	
6	import (
7		"context"
8		"errors"
9		"fmt"
10		"io"
11		"sync"
12	
13		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing"
14	
15		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/convert"
16		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/gitproto"
17		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/planner"
18	)
19	
20	// Params holds the inputs for an incremental relay execution.
21	type Params struct {
22		SourceConn    *gitproto.Conn
23		SourceService interface {
24			FetchPack(ctx context.Context, conn *gitproto.Conn, desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef, haves map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) (io.ReadCloser, error)
25		}
26		TargetPusher interface {
27			PushPack(ctx context.Context, cmds []gitproto.PushCommand, pack io.ReadCloser) error
28		}
29		DesiredRefs  map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef
30		TargetRefs   map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash
31		PushPlans    []planner.BranchPlan
32		MaxPackBytes int64
33		Verbose      bool
34		CanRelay     func(bool, bool, bool, []planner.BranchPlan) (bool, string)
35		CanTagRelay  func([]planner.BranchPlan) (bool, string)
36	}
37	
38	// Result holds the outcome of an incremental relay.
39	type Result struct {
40		Relay       bool
41		RelayMode   string
42		RelayReason string
43	}
44	
45	// Execute attempts the incremental relay strategy. Returns (result, nil) on
46	// success, or (zero, nil) if the strategy is not applicable. Errors indicate
47	// a relay was attempted but failed.
48	func Execute(ctx context.Context, p Params, cfg planner.PlanConfig) (Result, error) {
49		canRelay := p.CanRelay
50		if canRelay == nil {
51			return Result{}, errors.New("incremental strategy requires CanRelay")
52		}
53		if p.TargetPusher == nil {
54			return Result{}, errors.New("incremental strategy requires TargetPusher")
55		}
56		cmds := convert.PlansToPushCommands(p.PushPlans)
57		if ok, reason := canRelay(cfg.Force, cfg.Prune, false, p.PushPlans); ok {
58			desired := convert.DesiredRefsForPlans(p.DesiredRefs, p.PushPlans)
59			packReader, err := p.SourceService.FetchPack(ctx, p.SourceConn, desired, p.TargetRefs)
60			if err != nil {
61				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("fetch source pack: %w", err)
62			}
63			packReader = gitproto.LimitPackReader(packReader, p.MaxPackBytes)
64			packReader = closeOnce(packReader)
65			if err := p.TargetPusher.PushPack(ctx, cmds, packReader); err != nil {
66				_ = packReader.Close()
67				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("push target refs: %w", err)
68			}
69			_ = packReader.Close()
70			return Result{Relay: true, RelayMode: "incremental", RelayReason: reason}, nil
71		}
72	
73		if p.CanTagRelay == nil {
74			return Result{}, errors.New("incremental strategy requires CanTagRelay")
75		}
76		if ok, reason := p.CanTagRelay(p.PushPlans); ok {
77			desired := convert.DesiredRefsForPlans(p.DesiredRefs, p.PushPlans)
78			packReader, err := p.SourceService.FetchPack(ctx, p.SourceConn, desired, nil)
79			if err != nil {
80				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("fetch source tag pack: %w", err)
81			}
82			packReader = gitproto.LimitPackReader(packReader, p.MaxPackBytes)
83			packReader = closeOnce(packReader)
84			if err := p.TargetPusher.PushPack(ctx, cmds, packReader); err != nil {
85				_ = packReader.Close()
86				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("push target refs: %w", err)
87			}
88			_ = packReader.Close()
89			return Result{Relay: true, RelayMode: "incremental", RelayReason: reason}, nil
90		}
91	
92		return Result{}, nil
93	}
94	
95	type closeOnceReadCloser struct {
96		io.ReadCloser
97	
98		once sync.Once
99	}
100	
101	func (c *closeOnceReadCloser) Close() error {
102		var err error
103		c.once.Do(func() {
104			err = c.ReadCloser.Close()
105		})
106		if err != nil {
107			return fmt.Errorf("close pack reader: %w", err)
108		}
109		return nil
110	}
111	
112	func closeOnce(rc io.ReadCloser) io.ReadCloser {
113		if rc == nil {
114			return nil
115		}
116		if _, ok := rc.(*closeOnceReadCloser); ok {
117			return rc
118		}
119		return &closeOnceReadCloser{ReadCloser: rc}
120	}
121

1	// Package materialized implements the materialized fallback push strategy.
2	// This path fetches objects into local memory, then encodes and pushes them
3	// to the target. Used when relay is not safe.
4	package materialized
5	
6	import (
7		"context"
8		"errors"
9		"fmt"
10	
11		git "github.com/go-git/go-git/v6"
12		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing"
13		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/storer"
14	
15		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/convert"
16		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/gitproto"
17		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/planner"
18	)
19	
20	// Params holds the inputs for a materialized push.
21	type Params struct {
22		Store         storer.Storer
23		SourceConn    *gitproto.Conn
24		SourceService interface {
25			FetchToStore(ctx context.Context, store storer.Storer, conn *gitproto.Conn, desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef, haves map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) error
26		}
27		TargetPusher interface {
28			PushObjects(ctx context.Context, cmds []gitproto.PushCommand, store storer.Storer, hashes []plumbing.Hash) error
29		}
30		DesiredRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef
31		TargetRefs  map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash
32		PushPlans   []planner.BranchPlan
33		MaxObjects  int
34	}
35	
36	// DefaultMaxMaterializedObjects is the default safety limit for the materialized fallback path.
37	// Beyond this count, the in-memory object store would consume excessive memory.
38	// Fail early rather than OOM (issue #15).
39	const DefaultMaxMaterializedObjects = 500_000
40	
41	type executor struct {
42		ctx    context.Context
43		params Params
44	}
45	
46	// Execute runs the materialized fallback: ensures tag objects are local,
47	// computes the object closure, and pushes to the target.
48	func Execute(ctx context.Context, p Params) error {
49		if len(p.PushPlans) == 0 {
50			return nil
51		}
52		return (&executor{ctx: ctx, params: p}).run()
53	}
54	
55	func (e *executor) run() error {
56		if err := e.ensureTagObjects(); err != nil {
57			return fmt.Errorf("prepare local objects for push: %w", err)
58		}
59		hashes, err := e.collectObjectClosure()
60		if err != nil {
61			return fmt.Errorf("compute objects to push: %w", err)
62		}
63		if err := e.enforceObjectLimit(hashes); err != nil {
64			return err
65		}
66		return e.push(hashes)
67	}
68	
69	func (e *executor) ensureTagObjects() error {
70		tagDesired := make(map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef)
71		for _, plan := range e.params.PushPlans {
72			if plan.Kind != planner.RefKindTag {
73				continue
74			}
75			if d, ok := e.params.DesiredRefs[plan.TargetRef]; ok {
76				tagDesired[plan.TargetRef] = gitproto.DesiredRef{
77					SourceRef: d.SourceRef, TargetRef: d.TargetRef,
78					SourceHash: d.SourceHash, IsTag: true,
79				}
80			}
81		}
82		if len(tagDesired) == 0 {
83			return nil
84		}
85		err := e.params.SourceService.FetchToStore(e.ctx, e.params.Store, e.params.SourceConn, tagDesired, nil)
86		if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, git.NoErrAlreadyUpToDate) {
87			return fmt.Errorf("fetch tag objects to store: %w", err)
88		}
89		return nil
90	}
91	
92	func (e *executor) collectObjectClosure() ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {
93		objects := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(e.params.PushPlans))
94		for _, plan := range e.params.PushPlans {
95			if plan.Action == planner.ActionCreate || plan.Action == planner.ActionUpdate {
96				objects = append(objects, plan.SourceHash)
97			}
98		}
99		hashes, err := planner.ObjectsToPush(e.params.Store, objects, e.params.TargetRefs)
100		if err != nil {
101			return nil, fmt.Errorf("compute objects to push: %w", err)
102		}
103		return hashes, nil
104	}
105	
106	func (e *executor) enforceObjectLimit(hashes []plumbing.Hash) error {
107		maxObjects := effectiveMaxObjects(e.params.MaxObjects)
108		if len(hashes) <= maxObjects {
109			return nil
110		}
111		return fmt.Errorf(
112			"materialized push requires %d objects (limit %d); use bootstrap for large initial syncs",
113			len(hashes), maxObjects,
114		)
115	}
116	
117	func (e *executor) push(hashes []plumbing.Hash) error {
118		cmds := convert.PlansToPushCommands(e.params.PushPlans)
119		if e.params.TargetPusher == nil {
120			return errors.New("materialized strategy requires TargetPusher")
121		}
122		if err := e.params.TargetPusher.PushObjects(e.ctx, cmds, e.params.Store, hashes); err != nil {
123			return fmt.Errorf("push target refs: %w", err)
124		}
125		return nil
126	}
127	
128	func effectiveMaxObjects(limit int) int {
129		if limit > 0 {
130			return limit
131		}
132		return DefaultMaxMaterializedObjects
133	}
134	
```

50		for _, plan := range p.PushPlans {
51			switch plan.Action {
52			case planner.ActionCreate, planner.ActionUpdate:
53				updatePlans = append(updatePlans, plan)
54			case planner.ActionDelete:
55				deletePlans = append(deletePlans, plan)
56			case planner.ActionSkip, planner.ActionBlock, planner.ActionWarn:
57				// not applicable: replicate runs before any rejection downgrade,
58				// and skip/block plans never reach the executor.
59			}
60		}
61	
62		if len(updatePlans) > 0 {
63			desired := convert.DesiredRefsForPlans(p.DesiredRefs, updatePlans)
64			packReader, err := p.SourceService.FetchPack(ctx, p.SourceConn, desired, p.TargetRefs)
65			if err != nil {
66				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("fetch source pack: %w", err)
67			}
68			packReader = gitproto.LimitPackReader(packReader, p.MaxPackBytes)
69			packReader = closeOnce(packReader)
70			if err := p.TargetPusher.PushPack(ctx, convert.PlansToPushCommands(updatePlans), packReader); err != nil {
71				_ = packReader.Close()
72				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("push target refs: %w", err)
73			}
74			_ = packReader.Close()
75		}
76	
77		if len(deletePlans) > 0 {
78			if err := p.TargetPusher.PushCommands(ctx, convert.PlansToPushCommands(deletePlans)); err != nil {
79				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("delete target refs: %w", err)
80			}
81		}
82	
83		return Result{Relay: true, RelayMode: "replicate", RelayReason: "replicate-overwrite-relay"}, nil
84	}
85	
86	type closeOnceReadCloser struct {
87		io.ReadCloser
88	
89		once sync.Once
90	}
91	
92	func (c *closeOnceReadCloser) Close() error {
93		var err error
94		c.once.Do(func() {
95			err = c.ReadCloser.Close()
96		})
97		if err != nil {
98			return fmt.Errorf("close pack reader: %w", err)
99		}

450	
451	func planConfig(cfg Config) planner.PlanConfig {
452		return planner.PlanConfig{
453			Branches:           cfg.Branches,
454			Mappings:           cfg.Mappings,
455			IncludeTags:        cfg.IncludeTags,
456			AllRefs:            cfg.AllRefs,
457			ExcludeRefPrefixes: cfg.ExcludeRefPrefixes,
458			Force:              cfg.Force,
459			Prune:              cfg.Prune,
460		}
461	}
462	
463	// needsLocalSourceClosure reports whether the sync must populate the
464	// in-memory store with the full source closure before running. The fetch
465	// is required when:
466	//   - Force or prune is set: incremental relay is disabled, so the
467	//     materialized fallback will run and needs the closure.
468	//   - Any desired ref already exists on target at a different hash: a
469	//     branch fast-forward check will need ancestry data, or a tag retarget
470	//     will route to materialized.
471	//
472	// When all desired refs are either skips (target hash matches source) or
473	// creates (target hash is zero), incremental relay handles the push without
474	// the closure — the upfront fetch would just be wasted bandwidth, since
475	// relay does its own FetchPack on the source.
476	func needsLocalSourceClosure(
477		cfg Config,
478		desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef,
479		targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash,
480	) bool {
481		if cfg.Force || cfg.Prune {
482			return true
483		}
484		for targetRef, want := range desired {
485			targetHash := targetRefs[targetRef]
486			if targetHash.IsZero() {
487				continue
488			}
489			if targetHash == want.SourceHash {
490				continue
491			}
492			return true
493		}
494		return false
495	}
496	
497	// --- Session setup (issue #12) ---
498	
499	// syncSession holds the shared state for a sync operation, reducing
500	// setup duplication across Run, Bootstrap, Probe, and Fetch.
501	type syncSession struct {
502		cfg             Config
503		stats           *statsCollector
504		logger          *slog.Logger
505		sourceConn      *gitproto.Conn
506		sourceService   *gitproto.RefService
507		sourceRefMap    map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash
508		target          *targetSession
509		measurementDone func() Measurement
510		progress        *progressReporter
511		// rejections records target ng statuses; nil unless BestEffort.
512		rejections map[plumbing.ReferenceName]string
513	}
514	
515	// finish releases any resources owned by the session — currently the live
516	// progress ticker. Idempotent and safe to call from defer in callers that
517	// also produce results in the happy path.
518	func (s *syncSession) finish() {
519		if s.progress != nil {
520			s.progress.terminate()
521		}
522	}
523	
524	// notice surfaces a one-line human-readable event during a sync. When
525	// the live progress ticker is active it prints above the current frame;
526	// otherwise it falls back to plain stderr so the message is still seen
527	// when --progress is off or the destination is not a TTY.
528	func (s *syncSession) notice(msg string) {
529		if s.progress != nil {
530			s.progress.notify(msg)
531			return
532		}
533		fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, msg)
534	}
535	
536	type targetSession struct {
537		conn     *gitproto.Conn
538		adv      *packp.AdvRefs
539		refMap   map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash
540		features gitproto.TargetFeatures
541		policy   planner.RelayTargetPolicy
542		pusher   *gitproto.Pusher
543	}
544	
545	// newSession performs the shared setup: protocol validation, mapping validation,
546	// connection creation, and ref discovery.
547	func newSession(ctx context.Context, cfg Config, needTarget bool) (*syncSession, error) {
548		mode, err := validation.NormalizeProtocolMode(cfg.ProtocolMode)
549		if err != nil {
550			return nil, fmt.Errorf("normalize protocol mode: %w", err)
551		}
552		cfg.ProtocolMode = mode
553		switch cfg.Mode {
554		case "", modeSync:
555			cfg.Mode = modeSync
556		case modeReplicate:
557		default:
558			return nil, fmt.Errorf("unsupported operation mode %q", cfg.Mode)
559		}
560		if _, err := validation.ValidateMappings(cfg.Mappings, cfg.AllRefs); err != nil {
561			return nil, fmt.Errorf("validate mappings: %w", err)
562		}
563		if cfg.Mode == modeReplicate && cfg.Force {
564			return nil, errors.New("replicate does not support --force; use sync instead")
565		}
566		if needTarget {
567			if err := validation.ValidateEndpoints(cfg.Source.URL, cfg.Target.URL); err != nil {
568				return nil, fmt.Errorf("validate endpoints: %w", err)
569			}

BestEffort downgrades per-ref\n// receive-pack rejections to warnings; pack-level failures remain fatal.\ntype SyncPolicy struct {\n\tMode        OperationMode `json:\"mode\"`\n\tIncludeTags bool          `json:\"includeTags\"`\n\tForce       bool          `json:\"force\"`\n\tPrune       bool          `json:\"prune\"`\n\tBestEffort  bool          `json:\"bestEffort,omitempty\"`\n\tProtocol    ProtocolMode  `json:\"protocol\"`\n}",
  "new_string": "// SyncPolicy controls high-level sync behavior. BestEffort downgrades per-ref\n// receive-pack rejections to warnings; pack-level failures remain fatal.\n//\n// ForceWithLease and ForceBlind both allow non-fast-forward branch updates and\n// tag retargets; they differ in how the push command's expected-old value is\n// set. ForceWithLease sends the target tip captured at session start, so\n// receive-pack rejects updates where the target moved during the run (the\n// \"lease\"). ForceBlind sends a zero expected-old, telling receive-pack to\n// overwrite regardless of the current target value — matching `git push\n// --force` semantics. The two are mutually exclusive.\ntype SyncPolicy struct {\n\tMode           OperationMode `json:\"mode\"`\n\tIncludeTags    bool          `json:\"includeTags\"`\n\tForceWithLease bool          `json:\"forceWithLease,omitempty\"`\n\tForceBlind     bool          `json:\"forceBlind,omitempty\"`\n\tPrune          bool          `json:\"prune\"`\n\tBestEffort     bool          `json:\"bestEffort,omitempty\"`\n\tProtocol       ProtocolMode  `json:\"protocol\"`\n}"
}

58	func ProbeConfig(source Endpoint, sourceAuth EndpointAuth, target *Endpoint, targetAuth EndpointAuth, protocol ProtocolMode, includeTags, allRefs, collectStats bool, excludeRefPrefixes []string, httpClient *http.Client) Config {
59		cfg := syncer.Config{
60			Source:             ToSyncerEndpoint(source, sourceAuth),
61			HTTPClient:         httpClient,
62			IncludeTags:        includeTags,
63			AllRefs:            allRefs,
64			ExcludeRefPrefixes: append([]string(nil), excludeRefPrefixes...),
65			ShowStats:          collectStats,
66			ProtocolMode:       protocolString(protocol),
67		}
68		if target != nil {
69			cfg.Target = ToSyncerEndpoint(*target, targetAuth)
70		}
71		return Config{raw: cfg}
72	}
73	
74	func SyncConfig(source Endpoint, sourceAuth EndpointAuth, target Endpoint, targetAuth EndpointAuth, scope RefScope, policy SyncPolicy, collectStats, dryRun bool, httpClient *http.Client) Config {
75		return Config{raw: syncer.Config{
76			Source:                 ToSyncerEndpoint(source, sourceAuth),
77			Target:                 ToSyncerEndpoint(target, targetAuth),
78			HTTPClient:             httpClient,
79			Branches:               append([]string(nil), scope.Branches...),
80			Mappings:               ToValidationMappings(scope.Mappings),
81			AllRefs:                scope.AllRefs,
82			ExcludeRefPrefixes:     append([]string(nil), scope.ExcludeRefPrefixes...),
83			IncludeTags:            policy.IncludeTags,
84			DryRun:                 dryRun,
85			ShowStats:              collectStats,
86			Mode:                   operationModeString(policy.Mode),
87			Force:                  policy.Force,
88			Prune:                  policy.Prune,
89			BestEffort:             policy.BestEffort,
90			ProtocolMode:           protocolString(policy.Protocol),
91			MaterializedMaxObjects: syncer.DefaultMaterializedMaxObjects,
92		}}
93	}

95	func Probe(ctx context.Context, cfg Config) (syncer.ProbeResult, error) {
96		result, err := syncer.Probe(ctx, cfg.raw)
97		if err != nil {
98			return syncer.ProbeResult{}, err //nolint:wrapcheck // pass-through layer, caller wraps with context
99		}
100		return result, nil
101	}

104		result, err := syncer.Run(ctx, cfg.raw)
105		if err != nil {
106			return syncer.Result{}, err //nolint:wrapcheck // pass-through layer, caller wraps with context
107		}
108		return result, nil
109	}

111	func ToSyncerEndpoint(endpoint Endpoint, auth EndpointAuth) syncer.Endpoint {
112		return syncer.Endpoint{
113			URL:                    endpoint.URL,
114			Username:               auth.Username,
115			Token:                  auth.Token,
116			BearerToken:            auth.BearerToken,
117			SkipTLSVerify:          auth.SkipTLSVerify,
118			FollowInfoRefsRedirect: endpoint.FollowInfoRefsRedirect,
119		}
120	}

123		if mode == "" {
124			return string(ProtocolAuto)
125		}
126		return string(mode)
127	}
128

130		if mode == "" {
131			return string(ModeSync)
132		}
133		return string(mode)
134	}

136	func ToValidationMappings(mappings []RefMapping) []validation.RefMapping {
137		out := make([]validation.RefMapping, 0, len(mappings))
138		for _, mapping := range mappings {
139			out = append(out, validation.RefMapping{
140				Source: mapping.Source,
141				Target: mapping.Target,
142			})
143		}

1	package gitsync
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"errors"
6		"fmt"
7		"net/http"
8	
9		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/validation"
10		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internalbridge"
11	)
12	
13	// Options configures a Client. It is intentionally small in the first public cut.
14	type Options struct {
15		HTTPClient *http.Client
16		Auth       AuthProvider
17	}
18	
19	// Client provides the public orchestration API for git-sync.
20	type Client struct {
21		httpClient *http.Client
22		auth       AuthProvider
23	}
24	
25	// New constructs a new Client.
26	func New(opts Options) *Client {
27		return &Client{httpClient: opts.HTTPClient, auth: opts.Auth}
28	}
29	
30	// Probe inspects a source remote and optional target remote.
31	func (c *Client) Probe(ctx context.Context, req ProbeRequest) (ProbeResult, error) {
32		if err := req.Validate(); err != nil {
33			return ProbeResult{}, err
34		}
35		cfg, err := c.buildProbeConfig(ctx, req)
36		if err != nil {
37			return ProbeResult{}, err
38		}
39		result, err := internalbridge.Probe(ctx, cfg)
40		if err != nil {
41			return ProbeResult{}, fmt.Errorf("probe: %w", err)
42		}
43		return internalbridge.FromProbeResult(result), nil
44	}
45	
46	// Plan computes ref actions without pushing.
47	func (c *Client) Plan(ctx context.Context, req PlanRequest) (PlanResult, error) {
48		if err := req.Validate(); err != nil {
49			return PlanResult{}, err
50		}
51		cfg, err := c.buildSyncConfig(ctx, req.Source, req.Target, req.Scope, req.Policy, req.CollectStats, true)
52		if err != nil {
53			return PlanResult{}, err
54		}
55		result, err := internalbridge.Run(ctx, cfg)
56		if err != nil {
57			return PlanResult{}, fmt.Errorf("plan: %w", err)
58		}
59		return internalbridge.FromSyncResult(result), nil
60	}
61	
62	// Sync executes a sync between two remotes.
63	func (c *Client) Sync(ctx context.Context, req SyncRequest) (SyncResult, error) {
64		if err := req.Validate(); err != nil {
65			return SyncResult{}, err
66		}
67		cfg, err := c.buildSyncConfig(ctx, req.Source, req.Target, req.Scope, req.Policy, req.CollectStats, false)
68		if err != nil {
69			return SyncResult{}, err
70		}
71		result, err := internalbridge.Run(ctx, cfg)
72		if err != nil {
73			return SyncResult{}, fmt.Errorf("sync: %w", err)
74		}
75		return internalbridge.FromSyncResult(result), nil
76	}
77	
78	// Replicate executes source-authoritative relay-only replication between two remotes.
79	func (c *Client) Replicate(ctx context.Context, req SyncRequest) (SyncResult, error) {
80		req.Policy.Mode = ModeReplicate
81		return c.Sync(ctx, req)
82	}
83	
84	func (c *Client) buildProbeConfig(ctx context.Context, req ProbeRequest) (internalbridge.Config, error) {
85		sourceAuth, err := c.authFor(ctx, req.Source, SourceRole)
86		if err != nil {
87			return internalbridge.Config{}, err
88		}
89		var target *internalbridge.Endpoint
90		targetAuth := internalbridge.EndpointAuth{}
91		if req.Target != nil {
92			resolvedTargetAuth, err := c.authFor(ctx, *req.Target, TargetRole)
93			if err != nil {
94				return internalbridge.Config{}, err
95			}
96			target = ptr(bridgeEndpoint(*req.Target))
97			targetAuth = bridgeEndpointAuth(resolvedTargetAuth)
98		}
99		return internalbridge.ProbeConfig(
100			bridgeEndpoint(req.Source),
101			bridgeEndpointAuth(sourceAuth),
102			target,
103			targetAuth,
104			internalbridge.ProtocolMode(req.Protocol),
105			req.IncludeTags,
106			req.AllRefs,
107			req.CollectStats,
108			req.ExcludeRefPrefixes,
109			c.httpClient,
110		), nil
111	}
112	
113	func (c *Client) buildSyncConfig(ctx context.Context, source Endpoint, target Endpoint, scope RefScope, policy SyncPolicy, collectStats, dryRun bool) (internalbridge.Config, error) {
114		sourceAuth, err := c.authFor(ctx, source, SourceRole)
115		if err != nil {
116			return internalbridge.Config{}, err
117		}
118		targetAuth, err := c.authFor(ctx, target, TargetRole)
119		if err != nil {
120			return internalbridge.Config{}, err
121		}
122		return internalbridge.SyncConfig(
123			bridgeEndpoint(source),
124			bridgeEndpointAuth(sourceAuth),
125			bridgeEndpoint(target),
126			bridgeEndpointAuth(targetAuth),
127			bridgeScope(scope),
128			bridgePolicy(policy),
129			collectStats,
130			dryRun,
131			c.httpClient,
132		), nil
133	}
134	
135	func (c *Client) authFor(ctx context.Context, endpoint Endpoint, role EndpointRole) (EndpointAuth, error) {
136		if c.auth == nil {
137			return EndpointAuth{}, nil
138		}
139		auth, err := c.auth.AuthFor(ctx, endpoint, role)
140		if err != nil {
141			return EndpointAuth{}, fmt.Errorf("resolve auth for %s: %w", role, err)
142		}
143		return auth, nil
144	}
145	
146	func (r SyncRequest) Validate() error {
147		if r.Source.URL == "" {
148			return errors.New("source URL is required")
149		}
150		if r.Target.URL == "" {
151			return errors.New("target URL is required")
152		}
153		if err := validateOperationMode(r.Policy.Mode); err != nil {
154			return err
155		}
156		if _, err := validation.NormalizeProtocolMode(string(r.Policy.Protocol)); err != nil {
157			return fmt.Errorf("normalize protocol: %w", err)
158		}
159		if _, err := validation.ValidateMappings(validationMappings(r.Scope.Mappings), r.Scope.AllRefs); err != nil {
160			return fmt.Errorf("validate mappings: %w", err)
161		}
162		return nil
163	}
164	
165	func (r PlanRequest) Validate() error {
166		if r.Source.URL == "" {
167			return errors.New("source URL is required")
168		}
169		if r.Target.URL == "" {
170			return errors.New("target URL is required")
171		}
172		if err := validateOperationMode(r.Policy.Mode); err != nil {
173			return err
174		}
175		if _, err := validation.NormalizeProtocolMode(string(r.Policy.Protocol)); err != nil {
176			return fmt.Errorf("normalize protocol: %w", err)
177		}
178		if _, err := validation.ValidateMappings(validationMappings(r.Scope.Mappings), r.Scope.AllRefs); err != nil {
179			return fmt.Errorf("validate mappings: %w", err)
180		}
181		return nil
182	}
183	
184	func (r ProbeRequest) Validate() error {
185		if r.Source.URL == "" {
186			return errors.New("source URL is required")
187		}
188		if r.Target != nil && r.Target.URL == "" {
189			return errors.New("target URL is required when target endpoint is provided")
190		}
191		if _, err := validation.NormalizeProtocolMode(string(r.Protocol)); err != nil {
192			return fmt.Errorf("normalize protocol: %w", err)
193		}
194		return nil
195	}
196	
197	func bridgeEndpoint(ep Endpoint) internalbridge.Endpoint {
198		return internalbridge.Endpoint{
199			URL:                    ep.URL,
200			FollowInfoRefsRedirect: ep.FollowInfoRefsRedirect,
201		}
202	}
203	
204	func bridgeEndpointAuth(auth EndpointAuth) internalbridge.EndpointAuth {
205		return internalbridge.EndpointAuth{
206			Username:      auth.Username,
207			Token:         auth.Token,
208			BearerToken:   auth.BearerToken,
209			SkipTLSVerify: auth.SkipTLSVerify,
210		}
211	}
212	
213	func bridgeScope(scope RefScope) internalbridge.RefScope {
214		mappings := make([]internalbridge.RefMapping, 0, len(scope.Mappings))
215		for _, mapping := range scope.Mappings {
216			mappings = append(mappings, internalbridge.RefMapping{
217				Source: mapping.Source,
218				Target: mapping.Target,
219			})
220		}
221		return internalbridge.RefScope{
222			Branches:           append([]string(nil), scope.Branches...),
223			Mappings:           mappings,
224			AllRefs:            scope.AllRefs,
225			ExcludeRefPrefixes: append([]string(nil), scope.ExcludeRefPrefixes...),
226		}
227	}
228	
229	func bridgePolicy(policy SyncPolicy) internalbridge.SyncPolicy {
230		return internalbridge.SyncPolicy{
231			Mode:        internalbridge.OperationMode(policy.Mode),
232			IncludeTags: policy.IncludeTags,
233			Force:       policy.Force,
234			Prune:       policy.Prune,
235			BestEffort:  policy.BestEffort,
236			Protocol:    internalbridge.ProtocolMode(policy.Protocol),
237		}
238	}
239	
240	func validateOperationMode(mode OperationMode) error {
241		switch mode {
242		case "", ModeSync, ModeReplicate:
243			return nil
244		default:
245			return fmt.Errorf("unsupported operation mode %q", mode)
246		}
247	}
248	
249	func ptr[T any](v T) *T {
250		return &v
251	}
252	
253	func validationMappings(mappings []RefMapping) []validation.RefMapping {
254		out := make([]validation.RefMapping, 0, len(mappings))
255		for _, mapping := range mappings {
256			out = append(out, validation.RefMapping{
257				Source: mapping.Source,
258				Target: mapping.Target,
259			})
260		}
261		return out
262	}
263

37	type AdvancedOptions struct {
38		CollectStats           bool   `json:"collectStats"`
39		MeasureMemory          bool   `json:"measureMemory"`
40		Verbose                bool   `json:"verbose"`
41		Progress               bool   `json:"progress"`
42		MaxPackBytes           int64  `json:"maxPackBytes"`
43		TargetMaxPackBytes     int64  `json:"targetMaxPackBytes"`
44		MaterializedMaxObjects int    `json:"materializedMaxObjects"`
45		BootstrapStrategy      string `json:"bootstrapStrategy,omitempty"`

47	
48	// BootstrapStrategy values accepted by AdvancedOptions.BootstrapStrategy.
49	// Empty is treated as the default (first-parent).
50	//
51	// BootstrapStrategyTopo additionally requires the target to allow
52	// non-fast-forward updates under the refs/gitsync/ namespace, since
53	// successive checkpoints under topological ordering aren't guaranteed
54	// to be in an ancestor-descendant relationship and the internal temp
55	// ref may receive non-ff updates between batches. Major hosts allow
56	// this by default; only locked-down deployments need to be checked.
57	const (
58		BootstrapStrategyFirstParent = "first-parent"
59		BootstrapStrategyTopo        = "topo"
60	)
61	
62	// Validate rejects unknown values up front so callers don't have to
63	// wait for the deep bootstrap planning path to surface them. It runs
64	// on every request method regardless of whether the value will end
65	// up being consulted, since silently accepting a typo on a
66	// non-bootstrap path is worse than failing fast.
67	func (o AdvancedOptions) Validate() error {
68		switch o.BootstrapStrategy {
69		case "", BootstrapStrategyFirstParent, BootstrapStrategyTopo:
70			return nil
71		default:
72			return fmt.Errorf("unsupported bootstrap strategy %q (want %q or %q)",
73				o.BootstrapStrategy, BootstrapStrategyFirstParent, BootstrapStrategyTopo)
74		}
75	}

139		planReq := req

213	func (c *Client) buildProbeConfig(ctx context.Context, req ProbeRequest) (syncer.Config, error) {
214		source, err := c.resolveEndpoint(ctx, req.Source, gitsync.SourceRole)
215		if err != nil {
216			return syncer.Config{}, err
217		}
218		cfg := syncer.Config{
219			Source:             source,
220			HTTPClient:         c.httpClient,
221			IncludeTags:        req.IncludeTags,
222			AllRefs:            req.AllRefs,
223			ExcludeRefPrefixes: append([]string(nil), req.ExcludeRefPrefixes...),
224			ShowStats:          req.Options.CollectStats,
225			MeasureMemory:      req.Options.MeasureMemory,
226			Progress:           req.Options.Progress,
227			ProtocolMode:       protocolString(req.Protocol),
228			Verbose:            req.Options.Verbose,
229		}
230		if req.Target != nil {
231			target, err := c.resolveEndpoint(ctx, *req.Target, gitsync.TargetRole)
232			if err != nil {
233				return syncer.Config{}, err
234			}
235			cfg.Target = target
236		}
237		return cfg, nil
238	}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/convert/convert.go",
  "old_string": "// PlansToPushCommands converts planner BranchPlans directly to gitproto PushCommands.\nfunc PlansToPushCommands(plans []planner.BranchPlan) []gitproto.PushCommand {\n\tout := make([]gitproto.PushCommand, len(plans))\n\tfor i, p := range plans {\n\t\tout[i] = gitproto.PushCommand{\n\t\t\tName:   p.TargetRef,\n\t\t\tOld:    p.TargetHash,\n\t\t\tNew:    p.SourceHash,\n\t\t\tDelete: p.Action == planner.ActionDelete,\n\t\t}\n\t\tif out[i].Delete {\n\t\t\tout[i].New = plumbing.ZeroHash\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn out\n}",
  "new_string": "// PlansToPushCommands converts planner BranchPlans directly to gitproto PushCommands.\n//\n// When forceBlind is true, the expected-old hash is zeroed for non-delete\n// commands. This tells receive-pack to overwrite regardless of the current\n// target tip — matching `git push --force` semantics. Delete commands always\n// carry the captured target hash so the server can confirm what it is removing.\n//\n// When forceBlind is false (the default), commands carry the target hash\n// captured at session start; receive-pack rejects updates where the target\n// moved during the run, providing per-run `--force-with-lease` protection.\nfunc PlansToPushCommands(plans []planner.BranchPlan, forceBlind bool) []gitproto.PushCommand {\n\tout := make([]gitproto.PushCommand, len(plans))\n\tfor i, p := range plans {\n\t\tout[i] = gitproto.PushCommand{\n\t\t\tName:   p.TargetRef,\n\t\t\tOld:    p.TargetHash,\n\t\t\tNew:    p.SourceHash,\n\t\t\tDelete: p.Action == planner.ActionDelete,\n\t\t}\n\t\tif out[i].Delete {\n\t\t\tout[i].New = plumbing.ZeroHash\n\t\t} else if forceBlind {\n\t\t\tout[i].Old = plumbing.ZeroHash\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn out\n}"
}

77	
78				client := unstable.New(unstable.Options{
79					Auth: gitsync.StaticAuthProvider{Source: sourceAuth, Target: targetAuth},
80				})
81	
82				var (
83					result unstable.Result
84					err    error
85				)
86				ctx := cmd.Context()
87				switch {
88				case dryRun:
89					result, err = client.Plan(ctx, req)
90				case req.Policy.Mode == gitsync.ModeReplicate:
91					result, err = client.Replicate(ctx, req)
92				default:
93					result, err = client.Sync(ctx, req)
94				}
95				if err != nil {
96					return fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", name, err)
97				}
98				printOutput(jsonOutput, result)
99	
100				if !dryRun && result.Blocked > 0 {
101					return errors.New("one or more branches were skipped because the target was not fast-forwardable")
102				}
103				return nil
104			},
105		}
106	
107		addSourceEndpoint(cmd, &req.Source)
108		addTargetEndpoint(cmd, &req.Target)
109		addSourceAuth(cmd, &sourceAuth)
110		addTargetAuth(cmd, &targetAuth)
111	
112		cmd.Flags().StringVar(&branches, "branch", "", "comma-separated branch list; default is all source branches")
113		cmd.Flags().StringArrayVar(&mappings, "map", nil, "ref mapping in src:dst form; short names map branches, full refs map exact refs")
114		if name == "plan" {
115			cmd.Flags().Var(&modeValue, "mode", "operation mode: sync or replicate")
116		}
117		cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&req.Policy.IncludeTags, "tags", false, "mirror tags")
118		cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&req.Policy.ForceWithLease, "force-with-lease", false, "allow non-fast-forward branch updates and retarget tags; receive-pack rejects updates where the target moved during the run (lease captured at session start)")
119		cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&req.Policy.ForceBlind, "force-blind", false, "allow non-fast-forward branch updates and retarget tags; overwrite regardless of current target tip (matches `git push --force`)")
120		var legacyForce bool
121		cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&legacyForce, "force", false, "removed: pick --force-with-lease (previous semantics) or --force-blind (real overwrite)")
122		if err := cmd.Flags().MarkHidden("force"); err != nil {
123			panic(err)
124		}
125		cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&req.Policy.Prune, "prune", false, "delete managed target refs that no longer exist on source")
126		// Tag inclusion is now handled at the library level (AllRefs implies
127		// it in BuildDesiredRefs). Replicate keeps strict failure semantics —
128		// its contract is "target refs match source," so BestEffort is not
129		// bundled there; sync/plan get it for the best-effort UX.
130		var implies []*bool
131		usage := allRefsUsageBestEffort
132		if defaultMode == gitsync.ModeReplicate {
133			usage = allRefsUsageStrict
134		} else {
135			implies = append(implies, &req.Policy.BestEffort)
136		}
137		allRefsFlag(cmd, usage, &req.Scope.AllRefs, implies...)
138		excludeRefPrefixFlag(cmd, &req.Scope.ExcludeRefPrefixes)
139		cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&req.Options.CollectStats, "stats", false, "print transfer statistics")
140		cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&req.Options.MeasureMemory, "measure-memory", false, "sample elapsed time and Go heap usage")
141		cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&req.Options.Progress, "progress", false, "show live per-side throughput on stderr (TTY only)")
142		cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&jsonOutput, "json", false, "print JSON output")
143		cmd.Flags().IntVar(&req.Options.MaterializedMaxObjects, "materialized-max-objects", unstable.DefaultMaterializedMaxObjects, "abort non-relay materialized syncs above this many objects")
144		cmd.Flags().Int64Var(&req.Options.MaxPackBytes, "max-pack-bytes", 0, "abort bootstrap-relay push if the streamed source pack exceeds this many bytes")
145		cmd.Flags().Int64Var(&req.Options.TargetMaxPackBytes, "target-max-pack-bytes", 0, "target receive-pack body size limit; batches are planned and auto-subdivided to fit")

50	func TestAdvancedOptionsValidate(t *testing.T) {
51		t.Parallel()
52		cases := []struct {
53			name    string
54			opts    AdvancedOptions
55			wantErr bool
56		}{
57			{name: "empty strategy is the default", opts: AdvancedOptions{}, wantErr: false},
58			{name: "first-parent accepted", opts: AdvancedOptions{BootstrapStrategy: BootstrapStrategyFirstParent}, wantErr: false},
59			{name: "topo accepted", opts: AdvancedOptions{BootstrapStrategy: BootstrapStrategyTopo}, wantErr: false},
60			{name: "typo rejected at API edge", opts: AdvancedOptions{BootstrapStrategy: "topographic"}, wantErr: true},
61			{name: "case-sensitive: TOPO is not topo", opts: AdvancedOptions{BootstrapStrategy: "TOPO"}, wantErr: true},
62		}
63		for _, c := range cases {
64			t.Run(c.name, func(t *testing.T) {
65				t.Parallel()
66				err := c.opts.Validate()
67				if (err != nil) != c.wantErr {
68					t.Errorf("Validate() err=%v, wantErr=%v", err, c.wantErr)
69				}
70			})
71		}
72	}
73	
74	func TestClientRejectsUnknownStrategyBeforeIO(t *testing.T) {
75		t.Parallel()
76		// The reviewer's concern: an invalid value should fail at the
77		// API edge, not silently slip through on a non-bootstrap path
78		// (e.g. Probe) where bootstrap planning never runs.
79		c := New(Options{HTTPClient: &http.Client{}})
80		bad := AdvancedOptions{BootstrapStrategy: "unsupported"}
81		if _, err := c.Probe(context.Background(), ProbeRequest{
82			Source:  gitsync.Endpoint{URL: "https://source.example/repo.git"},
83			Options: bad,
84		}); err == nil {
85			t.Errorf("Probe with invalid bootstrap strategy should error")
86		}
87		if _, err := c.Sync(context.Background(), SyncRequest{
88			Source:  gitsync.Endpoint{URL: "https://source.example/repo.git"},
89			Target:  gitsync.Endpoint{URL: "https://target.example/repo.git"},
90			Options: bad,
91		}); err == nil {
92			t.Errorf("Sync with invalid bootstrap strategy should error")
93		}
94		if _, err := c.Bootstrap(context.Background(), BootstrapRequest{
95			Source:  gitsync.Endpoint{URL: "https://source.example/repo.git"},
96			Target:  gitsync.Endpoint{URL: "https://target.example/repo.git"},
97			Options: bad,
98		}); err == nil {
99			t.Errorf("Bootstrap with invalid bootstrap strategy should error")
100		}
101	}
102	
103	func TestBuildFetchConfigCopiesHaveHashesAtCallSite(t *testing.T) {
104		req := FetchRequest{
105			Source:     gitsync.Endpoint{URL: "https://source.example/repo.git"},
106			HaveHashes: []plumbing.Hash{plumbing.NewHash("aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa")},
107		}
108		cfg, err := New(Options{}).buildFetchConfig(context.Background(), req)

111		}
112		if cfg.Source.URL == "" {
113			t.Fatalf("source URL not set")
114		}
115	}

```
195	`--exclude-ref-prefix` subtracts from auto-discovery (branches, tags, and
196	`--all-refs` namespaces) and from prune scope, so excluded refs are left
197	alone entirely: not pulled from source, not pushed to target, not pruned
198	from target. Explicit `--map` entries are not subject to this filter.
199	
200	`replicate --all-refs` broadens the same scope but does NOT enable
201	best-effort. Replicate's contract is "target refs match source"; downgrading
202	rejected refs to warnings would let partial mirrors exit successfully,
203	which contradicts the command. Use `sync --all-refs` if you want
204	best-effort completeness against hostile targets.
205	
206	`sync --all-refs` blocks updates to non-branch refs (notes, pulls, custom
207	namespaces) by default — those refs don't generally form fast-forward
208	chains, so the same `--force` opt-in that retargets tags is required to
209	update them. `replicate` doesn't run that check; its overwrite contract
210	covers other-kind refs without `--force`.
211	
212	`SyncPolicy.BestEffort` is independent of scope and can be set without
213	`AllRefs` if a library caller wants per-ref warn semantics on a narrower
214	scope.
215	
216	Force source-side protocol v2:
217	
218	```bash
219	git-sync sync \
220	  --protocol v2 \
221	  <source-url> \
222	  <target-url>
223	```
224	
225	## HEAD / Default Branch
226	
227	git-sync surfaces the source's symref HEAD target — the source's default
228	branch — in result output:
229	
230	- JSON: `execution.sourceHead` (sync / plan / replicate / bootstrap); `sourceHead` (probe).
231	- Human: a `source-head: <ref>` line.
232	
233	```
234	$ git-sync probe --json <source-url> | jq .sourceHead
235	"refs/heads/main"
236	```
237	
238	**Bootstrap pushes the source HEAD's branch first.** When initializating a new 
239	repository, the default branch is based on the first push to a fresh repo (GitHub, GitLab,
240	others), this makes the mirror's default branch match the source
241	without any manual step. No flag needed — the ordering is always applied.
242	
243	git's wire protocol has no command for updating a remote symref, so for
244	hosts that *don't* infer the default from first-push (raw bare repos,
245	some self-hosted setups), the default branch has to be set out of band:
246	
247	1. **Match the default at init time** — `git init --bare --initial-branch=<source-default>` on the target before the first sync.
248	2. **Set HEAD post-sync** — `git symbolic-ref HEAD refs/heads/<source-default>` on the bare repo, or the host's API/UI (GitHub `PATCH /repos/{owner}/{repo}` with `default_branch`; GitLab `PUT /projects/:id` with `default_branch`).
249	
250	## JSON Output
251	
252	Add `--json` to any command to emit machine-readable output instead of the default text format.
253	
254	The JSON interface is stable:
255	
256	- keys use `camelCase`
257	- refs and hashes are serialized as strings, not raw byte arrays
258	- top-level keys include `plans`, `pushed`, `skipped`, `blocked`, `deleted`, `warned`, `dryRun`, `protocol`, and `stats`, plus `relay`, `relayMode`, `relayReason`, `batching`, `batchCount`, `plannedBatchCount`, and `tempRefs`
259	- each item in `plans` includes stable string fields such as `branch`, `sourceRef`, `targetRef`, `sourceHash`, `targetHash`, `kind`, `action`, and `reason`
260	- `execution.sourceHead` (sync/plan/replicate/bootstrap) and `sourceHead` (probe) carry the source's symref HEAD target when advertised
261	
262	## Auth
263	
264	For GitHub and similar providers, use basic auth with a token as the password.
265	
266	Auth is resolved in this order:
267	
268	- explicit CLI flags
269	- `GITSYNC_*` environment variables
270	- local `git credential fill` helper lookup for `http` and `https` remotes
271	- anonymous access
272	
273	Relevant variables:
274	
275	- `GITSYNC_SOURCE_TOKEN`
276	- `GITSYNC_TARGET_TOKEN`
277	- `GITSYNC_SOURCE_USERNAME` default: `git`
278	- `GITSYNC_TARGET_USERNAME` default: `git`
279	
280	Bearer auth is also available:
281	
282	- `GITSYNC_SOURCE_BEARER_TOKEN`
283	- `GITSYNC_TARGET_BEARER_TOKEN`
284	
285	That means local testing against a dummy GitHub repo can reuse your regular Git credential helper setup without passing tokens on every command.
286	
287	## Protocol Notes
288	
289	- Source-side discovery and fetch can use protocol v2 when supported. Push stays on the existing v1 `receive-pack` path. `--protocol auto` tries v2 first and falls back to v1. `--protocol v2` requires the source to negotiate v2.
290	- Source fetch advertises current target tip hashes as `have`, so reruns download less when source and target already share history.
291	- Branches are updated only when the target tip is an ancestor of the source tip, unless `--force` is set. Tags are immutable by default. Retargeting an existing tag requires `--force`. With `--prune`, managed target refs that are absent on source are deleted.
292	- If `sync` finds blocked refs, it exits non-zero before pushing anything.
293	- `--stats` adds per-service request, byte, want, have, and command counters to the output.
294	
295	For the deeper protocol-level walkthrough (smart HTTP, pkt-line, capability negotiation, sideband stripping, relay framing), see [protocol.md](protocol.md).
296	
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/docs/usage.md",
  "old_string": "## HEAD / Default Branch",
  "new_string": "## Force Updates and the Per-Run Lease\n\nNon-fast-forward updates and tag retargets are opt-in. git-sync exposes two\nflags that mirror `git push`'s force semantics:\n\n- **`--force-with-lease`** — allow non-fast-forward updates, but include the\n  target tip captured at session start as the push command's expected-old\n  value. If another writer moves the target between session start and the\n  push, receive-pack rejects the update with a \"remote ref does not match\n  expected old value\" error and the sync fails without clobbering the racing\n  write. The lease window is one sync run; git-sync keeps no state between\n  runs.\n- **`--force-blind`** — allow non-fast-forward updates and zero out the\n  expected-old, telling receive-pack to overwrite regardless of current\n  target value. Matches `git push --force` semantics. Use this when the\n  target was edited out-of-band and you intend to overwrite whatever is\n  there.\n\nThe two flags are mutually exclusive. Without either, divergent or\nnon-ancestor refs are reported as blocked and the sync exits non-zero\nbefore any push, so the lease check is a second line of defense against\nraces for users who opt into non-fast-forward updates.\n\n`bootstrap` and `replicate` do not accept force flags. Bootstrap seeds an\nempty target where every ref is a create; replicate is fast-forward-only by\ndesign.\n\nThe pre-0.5 `--force` flag is removed. Its semantics were lease-protected\n(it never sent a zero expected-old), so the closest direct replacement is\n`--force-with-lease`. `--force-blind` is new behavior with no pre-0.5\nanalog.\n\n## HEAD / Default Branch"
}
```
