  "new_string": "# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.\n\nThe format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/),\nand this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n### Changed (breaking)\n\n- `--force` is removed in favor of explicit `--force-with-lease` and `--force-blind`. The previous `--force` always sent the captured target tip as the expected-old, so it was lease-protected — `--force-with-lease` is the direct replacement. `--force-blind` is new and matches `git push --force` semantics (zero expected-old, overwrite regardless of current target value). `SyncPolicy.Force` becomes `SyncPolicy.ForceWithLease` and `SyncPolicy.ForceBlind` on the library API. Using the legacy `--force` flag now errors out with a migration hint ([#47](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/issues/47)).\n\n### Added\n\n- Per-run lease behavior documented under \"Force Updates and the Per-Run Lease\" in [docs/usage.md](docs/usage.md).\n\n## [0.4.3] - 2026-05-07"
}

1	# Changelog
2	
3	All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.
4	
5	The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/),
6	and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).
7	
8	## [0.4.3] - 2026-05-07
9	
10	### Added
11	
12	- `--bootstrap-strategy=topo` for merge-heavy repos: walks every reachable commit in deterministic topological order so batched bootstrap can place sub-pack boundaries inside side-branch ancestry ([#41](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/41))
13	- `--progress` for live per-side throughput across `sync`, `replicate`, `bootstrap`, and `fetch`, with rolling-window rate, hostname-aware labels, inline pack subdivision, and an end-of-run summary under `--stats` ([#37](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/37))
14	- Per-subcommand `--help` after the CLI moved to cobra; bare `git-sync` lists subcommands on stdout instead of printing the full usage block as an error ([#35](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/35))
15	- README cover image and embedded demo videos for `git-sync plan` and `git-sync sync` ([#30](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/30), [#31](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/31))
```

func (e UnpackStatusErr) Error() string {
	return fmt.Sprintf("unpack error: %s", e.Status)
}

// CommandStatusErr is the error returned when the command status is not ok.
type CommandStatusErr struct {
	ReferenceName plumbing.ReferenceName
	Status        string
}

// Error implements the error interface.
func (e CommandStatusErr) Error() string {
	return fmt.Sprintf("command error on %s: %s", e.ReferenceName.String(), e.Status)
}

// ReportStatus is a report status message, as used in the git-receive-pack
// process whenever the 'report-status' capability is negotiated.
type ReportStatus struct {
	UnpackStatus    string
	CommandStatuses []*CommandStatus
}

// NewReportStatus creates a new ReportStatus message.
func NewReportStatus() *ReportStatus {
	return &ReportStatus{}
}

// Error returns the first error if any.
func (s *ReportStatus) Error() error {
	if s.UnpackStatus != ok {
		return UnpackStatusErr{s.UnpackStatus}
	}

	for _, cs := range s.CommandStatuses {
		if err := cs.Error(); err != nil {
			// XXX: Here, we only return the first error following canonical
			// Git behavior.
			return err
		}
	}

	return nil
}

// Encode writes the report status to a writer.
func (s *ReportStatus) Encode(w io.Writer) error {
	if _, err := pktline.Writef(w, "unpack %s\n", s.UnpackStatus); err != nil {
		return err
	}
--

type trackingReadCloser struct {
	io.ReadCloser

	closed bool
}

func (r *trackingReadCloser) Close() error {
	r.closed = true
	if r.ReadCloser != nil {
		return r.ReadCloser.Close()
	}
	return nil
}

type gatedReadCloser struct {
	first   []byte
	second  string
	release <-chan struct{}
	stage   int
	closed  bool
}

func (r *gatedReadCloser) Read(p []byte) (int, error) {
	switch r.stage {
	case 0:
		r.stage = 1
		return copy(p, r.first), nil
	case 1:
		<-r.release
		r.stage = 2
		return copy(p, r.second), nil
	default:
		return 0, io.EOF
	}
}

func (r *gatedReadCloser) Close() error {
	r.closed = true
	return nil
}

> AGENT

All green. Summary of what landed:

- **Renamed `--force` → `--force-with-lease`** for the existing lease-protected behavior (sends captured target tip as expected-old; receive-pack rejects if the target moved during the run).
- **Added `--force-blind`** as the real `git push --force` equivalent — zeros expected-old, overwrites regardless of current target value.
- **Old `--force` errors out** with a migration hint pointing to the two replacements; flags are mutually exclusive (both at once errors).
- **API rename**: `SyncPolicy.Force` → `SyncPolicy.ForceWithLease` + `SyncPolicy.ForceBlind`; same for internal `syncer.Config`. New `Config.ForceAny()` helper at internal call sites for the "either flag set" sense.
- **Plumbing**: `convert.PlansToPushCommands(plans, forceBlind bool)` zeros `Old` for non-delete commands when blind. Threaded through `incremental.Params.ForceBlind` and `materialized.Params.ForceBlind`. Bootstrap and replicate pass `false` (both reject force flags entirely).
- **Lease-failure hint**: `annotateLeaseFailure` in `internal/gitproto/push.go` wraps `CommandStatusErr` with "target ref X moved or differs from session start; rerun, or use --force-blind to overwrite" when the server's ng reason matches stale-info / fetch-first / non-fast-forward / does-not-match patterns.
- **Docs**: new "Force Updates and the Per-Run Lease" section in `docs/usage.md` and Unreleased entry in `CHANGELOG.md` flagging the breaking change.
- **Tests**: convert/`PlansToPushCommands` covers both modes; CLI tests for legacy `--force` rejection, mutual exclusion, and replicate-with-force-flag rejection; `annotateLeaseFailure` table test.

Full short test suite passes.

# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).

## [0.4.3] - 2026-05-07

### Added

- `--bootstrap-strategy=topo` for merge-heavy repos: walks every reachable commit in deterministic topological order so batched bootstrap can place sub-pack boundaries inside side-branch ancestry ([#41](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/41))
- `--progress` for live per-side throughput across `sync`, `replicate`, `bootstrap`, and `fetch`, with rolling-window rate, hostname-aware labels, inline pack subdivision, and an end-of-run summary under `--stats` ([#37](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/37))
- Per-subcommand `--help` after the CLI moved to cobra; bare `git-sync` lists subcommands on stdout instead of printing the full usage block as an error ([#35](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/35))
- README cover image and embedded demo videos for `git-sync plan` and `git-sync sync` ([#30](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/30), [#31](https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/pull/31))
```

I have enough to finish the review.

---

Review findings — efficiency only:

**internal/convert/convert.go:80-83** — The `forceBlind` branch adds a single branch + one `plumbing.ZeroHash` assignment inside the existing per-plan loop. The `else if` short-circuits cleanly when `forceBlind` is false (one extra predictable branch per iteration, no allocation, no work). Hot path is unaffected in practice. No action needed.

**internal/syncer/syncer.go:472 (`ForceAny`)** — Called at 6 sites across `syncer.go` (lines 459, 487, 698, 776, 781, 939, 963) plus 2 test files. All calls are once-per-run at session-level decision points (planConfig, needsLocalSourceClosure, CanBootstrapRelay, RelayFallbackReason, Bootstrap entry, replicate guard). None are inside per-ref or per-plan loops. The function is a trivial `||` over two bools and inlinable by the compiler — caching would be premature.

**internal/gitproto/push.go:108-127 (`annotateLeaseFailure`)** — `strings.ToLower` + 4× `strings.Contains` only runs after `errors.As` confirms a `CommandStatusErr` and only on the error path in `sendReceivePack` (line 171). Successful pushes return at line 173 without touching it. The 4 substrings are short (≤16 chars) against typically <100-char status strings; this is well under any threshold where a regex or `strings.EqualFold` per-substring would help. Acceptable as-is.

**internal/gitproto/push_test.go:383-415** — Pure unit tests over `annotateLeaseFailure` with table-driven cases; no servers, no network. Appropriate scope.

**cmd/git-sync/main_test.go:557-601** — New tests use `http://127.0.0.1:1/...` so flag parsing/validation fails before any dial. No server bloat.

**Allocations** — `PlansToPushCommands` still allocates a single `make([]gitproto.PushCommand, len(plans))` regardless of `forceBlind`; no new slices/maps were added on the `forceBlind=false` path. The `legacyForce` bool in syncplan.go and bench main.go is a single stack bool per command construction — negligible.

Overall: a small, clean diff. No real efficiency issues to fix.

```
Confirmed: go-git's `CommandStatusErr.Status` is a free-form string with no constants — string-matching is the only option short of upstream changes. Now compiling the review.

---

**Findings**

**1. Redundant state — `ForceWithLease` + `ForceBlind` bools vs. enum** — `types.go:99-107`, `internal/syncer/syncer.go:82-83`, `internalbridge/config.go:49-55`. The pair-of-bools shape encodes three valid states (none / lease / blind) plus one invalid (both set) that requires a runtime check in three places (see #3). A `ForceMode` enum (`ForceNone | ForceWithLease | ForceBlind`) makes the invariant unrepresentable and lets `ForceAny()` become `Force != ForceNone`. **Against:** the bool shape composes well with cobra/flag's `BoolVar` and the JSON struct stays additive (zero-value compatible). **For (stronger):** the new `ForceAny()` helper at `syncer.go:467` is a tell that the bools don't model the domain — and three identical mutual-exclusion checks are exactly the cost of the wider state space. Recommend an enum on the public `SyncPolicy` and `Config`; CLI layer can keep two flags and translate.

**2. Parameter sprawl — `PlansToPushCommands(plans, forceBlind bool)`** — `internal/convert/convert.go:69`. A bare bool added to a converter that previously took only data is a smell — eight call sites now pass a literal `false` (`bootstrap.go:179,489,674,684`, `replicate.go:70,78`). Cleanest fix: carry intent on `planner.BranchPlan` itself (e.g., `ForceBlind bool` per plan, or `ExpectedOld plumbing.Hash` already zeroed by the planner), so the converter stays a pure mapper and bootstrap/replicate don't need to know about the flag at all. An options struct is the middle option but doesn't remove the literal-`false` noise. Lean toward moving the decision into the planner.

**3. Copy-paste — mutex check in three places + legacy rejection in two** — `cmd/git-sync-bench/main.go:128`, `cmd/git-sync/syncplan.go:46-49`, `internal/syncer/syncer.go:569-571`. Three identical "mutually exclusive" checks; two identical "--force has been removed" rejections (`bench main.go:125`, `syncplan.go:45`). `newSession` already validates and is the chokepoint every entry path hits — both checks belong there alone. The CLI layers can lean on the library's error and stop duplicating the rule. The bench tool can call the same validator. Justification for duplication ("fail before parsing flags finishes") is weak: a usage error from the library surfaces the same way.

**4. Leaky abstraction — `ForceBlind` on strategy `Params`** — `internal/strategy/incremental/incremental.go:34`, `internal/strategy/materialized/materialized.go:34`. The strategies don't *use* `ForceBlind` for anything except forwarding it to `PlansToPushCommands`. If the planner produced plans with the intended `ExpectedOld` already set (#2), strategies wouldn't need the field. As written it leaks a CLI/policy concept two layers down. Keep it in convert (or planner) and drop from strategy Params.

**5. Stringly-typed `annotateLeaseFailure`** — `internal/gitproto/push.go:108-124`. Verified upstream: go-git v6 `packp.CommandStatusErr.Status` is a raw string with no sentinel constants (`report_status.go:31`). String matching is currently the only option, so this isn't avoidable — but the four substrings should be package-level `var leaseFailureMarkers = []string{...}` for testability and to keep the match list in one place. The lowercase-then-contains pattern is fine.

**6. Nested conditionals** — none new. `annotateLeaseFailure` is flat (early-return then one compound `if`); strategy/syncer changes are single-line additions.

**7. Unnecessary comments / doc bloat**
- **`internal/convert/convert.go:63-72`** — the 9-line doc on `PlansToPushCommands` re-explains lease vs. blind, already documented on `SyncPolicy`. If `forceBlind` goes away (#2) the comment goes with it; otherwise trim to one line referencing `SyncPolicy`.
- **`types.go:96-103`** — the SyncPolicy doc is load-bearing for public API users; keep it but consider moving the implementation detail ("sends the target tip captured at session start") to internal docs — public API consumers care about *behavior*, not wire format.
- **`cmd/git-sync/syncplan.go:46`** — the inline `errors.New` message is a 200-char paragraph explaining migration semantics. Move to a `const` or a `usage.md` link; this will rot the first time the flag changes again.
- **`internal/syncer/syncer.go:469-472`** — `ForceAny` comment is fine, but the function exists only to paper over #1; if you adopt the enum it disappears.
- **Migration references** — "Pre-0.5 --force" in `docs/usage.md:236-239` is appropriate (release notes context). The hidden `--force` flag at `syncplan.go:120-122` is right to keep for one release, then delete; add a TODO with a target version.

Now I have enough to finalize my findings.

**Code reuse findings**

1. **cmd/git-sync/syncplan.go:121-124 — `MarkHidden` panic-on-err pattern.** Only one use site in the repo; no shared helper exists. `cmd.Flags().MarkHidden` only ever returns an error when the flag isn't found, so this could be inlined to `_ = cmd.Flags().MarkHidden("force")` instead of panicking. More importantly, **pflag's `MarkDeprecated(name, msg)`** (`/Users/soph/go/pkg/mod/github.com/spf13/pflag@v1.0.10/flag.go:434`) was designed exactly for "this flag was removed, print a deprecation message" — it makes the flag hidden AND prints the message when used, eliminating both the `MarkHidden` panic dance and the manual `if legacyForce { return errors.New("--force has been removed...") }` block.

2. **The mutually-exclusive validation (syncer.go:569, syncplan.go:48, cmd/git-sync-bench/main.go:128).** Cobra v1.10.2 (in go.mod) ships **`(*Command).MarkFlagsMutuallyExclusive`** at `/Users/soph/go/pkg/mod/github.com/spf13/cobra@v1.10.2/flag_groups.go:65`. Calling `cmd.MarkFlagsMutuallyExclusive("force-with-lease", "force-blind")` in syncplan.go removes the manual RunE check and produces the same error. The bench command uses raw `flag.FlagSet` so it can't use it, but the syncer.go runtime check should stay as the library-level guard. Recommend: replace syncplan.go check with `MarkFlagsMutuallyExclusive`, keep syncer.go as the library guard, leave bench alone.

3. **`Config.ForceAny()` helper (internal/syncer/syncer.go:467).** No similar predicate-on-Config pattern exists elsewhere in the repo — `Config` has no other helper methods. This is novel and reasonable; nothing to swap.

4. **`annotateLeaseFailure` in internal/gitproto/push.go:108-125.** No existing status-string matching or error-wrapping utility lives in `internal/gitproto/` — this is the only place that inspects `packp.CommandStatusErr.Status`. The `strings.ToLower` + multi-`strings.Contains` pattern doesn't appear elsewhere in the repo. Nothing to swap.

5. **Legacy `--force` rejection (syncplan.go RunE + bench/main.go:125-127).** No shared "removed flag" helper exists; only two call sites with distinct flag libraries (cobra vs stdlib `flag`). The most idiomatic fix in cobra-land is to delete the `legacyForce` variable entirely and replace it with **`pflag.FlagSet.MarkDeprecated("force", "use --force-with-lease or --force-blind")`** — which the existing `cmd.Flags()` already supports. The bench path stays as-is (stdlib `flag` has no equivalent).

**Net recommendations:** swap syncplan.go's hidden-flag + RunE legacy check for `pflag.MarkDeprecated`; swap syncplan.go's mutex RunE check for cobra's `MarkFlagsMutuallyExclusive`. The other helpers (`ForceAny`, `annotateLeaseFailure`) have no existing equivalents and should stay.

Match\n// is case-insensitive. CommandStatusErr.Status is a free-form string in go-git,\n// so substring matching is the only option absent upstream sentinels.\nvar leaseFailureMarkers = []string{\n\t\"stale info\",\n\t\"fetch first\",\n\t\"non-fast-forward\",\n\t\"does not match\",\n}\n\n// annotateLeaseFailure wraps a lease-failure CommandStatusErr with a retry/\n// override hint. Other receive-pack errors pass through unchanged.\nfunc annotateLeaseFailure(err error) error {\n\tvar cs *packp.CommandStatusErr\n\tif !errors.As(err, &cs) {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tstatus := strings.ToLower(cs.Status)\n\tfor _, marker := range leaseFailureMarkers {\n\t\tif strings.Contains(status, marker) {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"%w (target ref %s moved or differs from session start; rerun, or use --force-blind to overwrite)\", err, cs.ReferenceName)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn err\n}"

can you give me a short pr descriptikon

> AGENT

## Clarify force semantics: `--force-with-lease` and `--force-blind`

Closes #47.

The old `--force` was always lease-protected — it sent the captured session-start target tip as the push command's expected-old, so receive-pack rejected updates where the target had moved during the run. The name oversold the danger: it never matched `git push --force`'s raw clobber.

Replace it with two explicit flags that mirror git's surface:

- **`--force-with-lease`** — previous behavior (allow non-FF, captured tip as expected-old; server rejects on lease miss).
- **`--force-blind`** — new path (zero expected-old, overwrite regardless of current target value; matches `git push --force`).

The two flags are mutually exclusive. Legacy `--force` errors out with a migration hint. `bootstrap` and `replicate` continue to reject force flags entirely. Pre-0.5, so hard switch — no silent semantics flip.

On the API side, `SyncPolicy.Force` splits into `ForceWithLease` + `ForceBlind`. `convert.PlansToPushCommands` takes a `forceBlind` bool; sync's incremental and materialized strategies plumb it through.

Also: when receive-pack returns a lease-failure `ng` (stale info / fetch first / non-fast-forward / does not match), the error is wrapped with a "target ref X moved or differs from session start; rerun, or use --force-blind to overwrite" hint.

### Notes

- Docs: new "Force Updates and the Per-Run Lease" section in `docs/usage.md`.
- CHANGELOG left untouched — to be updated at release time.
- Tests cover both convert modes, legacy `--force` rejection, mutual exclusion, replicate-with-force rejection, and the lease-failure annotation.

> DEVELOPER

Findings:

1. High: `BestEffort` currently defeats the new `--force-with-lease` guarantee. In [internal/syncer/syncer.go](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/syncer/syncer.go:634), any best-effort run installs `OnRejection` for all per-ref `ng` statuses. Then [internal/gitproto/push.go](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/gitproto/push.go:172) skips `report.Error()` entirely when that callback is present, so lease-mismatch statuses get downgraded to warnings instead of failing the run. That means `sync --all-refs --force-with-lease` can exit successfully after a concurrent target update, which contradicts the documented lease semantics.

2. Medium: the public and unstable APIs no longer reject invalid force combinations at the request edge. [client.go](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/client.go:146) validates URLs/protocol/mappings, but never rejects `ForceWithLease && ForceBlind` or `ModeReplicate` plus force flags. The unstable client has the same issue: [unstable/client.go](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/unstable/client.go:152) goes straight into `buildSyncConfig`, and the actual rejection only happens later in [internal/syncer/syncer.go](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/syncer/syncer.go:569). That’s a regression from the stated “fail before I/O / at the API edge” intent, and it also means custom auth providers may run before the request is rejected.

3. Low: the updated docs now describe `replicate` incorrectly. [docs/usage.md](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/docs/usage.md:248) says “replicate is fast-forward-only by design,” but the implementation and tests still treat it as overwrite-oriented replication; for example [internal/syncer/integration_test.go](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/syncer/integration_test.go:2576) explicitly asserts divergent tag overwrite succeeds in replicate mode. That wording will mislead users choosing between `sync` and `replicate`.

I also tried to run `go test ./internal/gitproto ./internal/syncer ./cmd/git-sync`, but the sandbox blocks `httptest` from binding a local port, so I couldn’t use test execution to validate these paths here.

423			case ActionWarn, ActionSkip, ActionBlock:
424			}
425		}
426		return pushed, deleted
427	}

  "old_string": "// leaseFailureMarkers are receive-pack ng reason substrings that indicate the\n// captured target tip didn't match what was on the server at push time. Match\n// is case-insensitive. CommandStatusErr.Status is a free-form string in go-git,\n// so substring matching is the only option absent upstream sentinels.\nvar leaseFailureMarkers = []string{\n\t\"stale info\",\n\t\"fetch first\",\n\t\"non-fast-forward\",\n\t\"does not match\",\n}\n\n// annotateLeaseFailure wraps a lease-failure CommandStatusErr with a retry/\n// override hint. Other receive-pack errors pass through unchanged.\nfunc annotateLeaseFailure(err error) error {\n\tvar cs *packp.CommandStatusErr\n\tif !errors.As(err, &cs) {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tstatus := strings.ToLower(cs.Status)\n\tfor _, marker := range leaseFailureMarkers {\n\t\tif strings.Contains(status, marker) {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"%w (target ref %s moved or differs from session start; rerun, or use --force-blind to overwrite)\", err, cs.ReferenceName)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn err\n}",
  "new_string": "// leaseFailureMarkers are receive-pack ng reason substrings that indicate the\n// captured target tip didn't match what was on the server at push time. Match\n// is case-insensitive. CommandStatusErr.Status is a free-form string in go-git,\n// so substring matching is the only option absent upstream sentinels.\nvar leaseFailureMarkers = []string{\n\t\"stale info\",\n\t\"fetch first\",\n\t\"non-fast-forward\",\n\t\"does not match\",\n}\n\n// IsLeaseFailure reports whether a receive-pack ng reason indicates the\n// captured target tip no longer matched at push time. Callers that downgrade\n// per-ref rejections to warnings (BestEffort) must still treat these as fatal\n// to preserve --force-with-lease semantics.\nfunc IsLeaseFailure(status string) bool {\n\tlowered := strings.ToLower(status)\n\tfor _, marker := range leaseFailureMarkers {\n\t\tif strings.Contains(lowered, marker) {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}\n\n// annotateLeaseFailure wraps a lease-failure CommandStatusErr with a retry/\n// override hint. Other receive-pack errors pass through unchanged.\nfunc annotateLeaseFailure(err error) error {\n\tvar cs *packp.CommandStatusErr\n\tif !errors.As(err, &cs) {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tif !IsLeaseFailure(cs.Status) {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"%w (target ref %s moved or differs from session start; rerun, or use --force-blind to overwrite)\", err, cs.ReferenceName)\n}"
}

Without this, a sync with\n// both --force-with-lease and --all-refs (which implies BestEffort) would\n// silently treat a concurrent target update as a warning, defeating the lease.\nfunc (s *syncSession) leaseFailureError() error {\n\tif len(s.rejections) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tvar refs []string\n\tfor name, status := range s.rejections {\n\t\tif gitproto.IsLeaseFailure(status) {\n\t\t\trefs = append(refs, name.String())\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif len(refs) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tsort.Strings(refs)\n\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"lease failure on %d ref(s) (%s) — target moved during sync; rerun, or use --force-blind to overwrite\", len(refs), strings.Join(refs, \", \"))\n}\n\n// applyRejections downgrades plans whose ref was rejected by the target to\n// ActionWarn and returns the count.\nfunc (s *syncSession) applyRejections(plans []BranchPlan) int {"

94	// SyncPolicy controls high-level sync behavior. BestEffort downgrades per-ref
95	// receive-pack rejections to warnings; pack-level failures remain fatal.
96	//
97	// ForceWithLease and ForceBlind both allow non-fast-forward branch updates and
98	// tag retargets; they differ in how the push command's expected-old value is
99	// set. ForceWithLease sends the target tip captured at session start, so
100	// receive-pack rejects updates where the target moved during the run (the
101	// "lease"). ForceBlind sends a zero expected-old, telling receive-pack to
102	// overwrite regardless of the current target value — matching `git push
103	// --force` semantics. The two are mutually exclusive.
104	type SyncPolicy struct {
105		Mode           OperationMode `json:"mode"`
106		IncludeTags    bool          `json:"includeTags"`
107		ForceWithLease bool          `json:"forceWithLease,omitempty"`
108		ForceBlind     bool          `json:"forceBlind,omitempty"`

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/types.go",
  "old_string": "// SyncPolicy controls high-level sync behavior. BestEffort downgrades per-ref\n// receive-pack rejections to warnings; pack-level failures remain fatal.\n//\n// ForceWithLease and ForceBlind both allow non-fast-forward branch updates and\n// tag retargets; they differ in how the push command's expected-old value is\n// set. ForceWithLease sends the target tip captured at session start, so\n// receive-pack rejects updates where the target moved during the run (the\n// \"lease\"). ForceBlind sends a zero expected-old, telling receive-pack to\n// overwrite regardless of the current target value — matching `git push\n// --force` semantics. The two are mutually exclusive.\ntype SyncPolicy struct {\n\tMode           OperationMode `json:\"mode\"`\n\tIncludeTags    bool          `json:\"includeTags\"`\n\tForceWithLease bool          `json:\"forceWithLease,omitempty\"`\n\tForceBlind     bool          `json:\"forceBlind,omitempty\"`\n\tPrune          bool          `json:\"prune\"`\n\tBestEffort     bool          `json:\"bestEffort,omitempty\"`\n\tProtocol       ProtocolMode  `json:\"protocol\"`\n}",
  "new_string": "// SyncPolicy controls high-level sync behavior. BestEffort downgrades per-ref\n// receive-pack rejections to warnings; pack-level failures remain fatal.\n//\n// ForceWithLease and ForceBlind both allow non-fast-forward branch updates and\n// tag retargets; they differ in how the push command's expected-old value is\n// set. ForceWithLease sends the target tip captured at session start, so\n// receive-pack rejects updates where the target moved during the run (the\n// \"lease\"). ForceBlind sends a zero expected-old, telling receive-pack to\n// overwrite regardless of the current target value — matching `git push\n// --force` semantics. The two are mutually exclusive.\ntype SyncPolicy struct {\n\tMode           OperationMode `json:\"mode\"`\n\tIncludeTags    bool          `json:\"includeTags\"`\n\tForceWithLease bool          `json:\"forceWithLease,omitempty\"`\n\tForceBlind     bool          `json:\"forceBlind,omitempty\"`\n\tPrune          bool          `json:\"prune\"`\n\tBestEffort     bool          `json:\"bestEffort,omitempty\"`\n\tProtocol       ProtocolMode  `json:\"protocol\"`\n}\n\n// Validate enforces SyncPolicy invariants at the request edge.\nfunc (p SyncPolicy) Validate() error {\n\tif p.ForceWithLease && p.ForceBlind {\n\t\treturn errors.New(\"ForceWithLease and ForceBlind are mutually exclusive\")\n\t}\n\tif p.Mode == ModeReplicate && (p.ForceWithLease || p.ForceBlind) {\n\t\treturn errors.New(\"replicate does not support force flags; use sync instead\")\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

All three findings addressed in `583fcf1a`:

1. **BestEffort + lease (high)** — `gitproto.IsLeaseFailure` exported; `syncer.leaseFailureError` runs after `finalizeCounts` and escalates lease-class rejections to fatal regardless of BestEffort. `sync --all-refs --force-with-lease` now fails when the target moves, instead of silently downgrading to a warning.

2. **Request-edge validation (medium)** — `SyncPolicy.Validate` added; called from `gitsync.SyncRequest.Validate`, `gitsync.PlanRequest.Validate`, and `unstable.Client.{Sync,Plan,Replicate}`. The syncer-level check stays as defense-in-depth for direct `syncer.Config` callers (tests).

3. **Replicate doc wording (low)** — rewritten. Replicate's contract is source-authoritative overwrite (divergent refs retargeted unconditionally), not fast-forward-only; force flags are unnecessary rather than gated.

Tests: `TestLeaseFailureErrorEscalatesPastBestEffort` covers (1); the existing `TestValidateRequests` got two new cases for (2). Full short test suite green.

> DEVELOPER

One remaining finding:

1. Medium: lease-failure escalation is now applied unconditionally to every best-effort rejection, even when the run did not use `--force-with-lease`. [internal/syncer/syncer.go](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/syncer/syncer.go:433) treats any `gitproto.IsLeaseFailure(status)` rejection as fatal, and both sync and replicate call it unconditionally after `finalizeCounts` at [internal/syncer/syncer.go](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/syncer/syncer.go:825) and [internal/syncer/syncer.go](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/syncer/syncer.go:922). But `IsLeaseFailure` currently matches broad strings like `"non-fast-forward"` and `"fetch first"` in [internal/gitproto/push.go](/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/gitproto/push.go:112). That is correct for `--force-with-lease`, but wrong for `--force-blind` or non-force best-effort runs: those same server messages can reflect ordinary policy rejection rather than a stale lease. As written, `sync --all-refs --force-blind` can fail fatally on a rejection that the docs describe as warnable best-effort behavior, even though no lease semantics are in effect. This check should be gated on `s.cfg.ForceWithLease` or otherwise distinguish true lease-mode pushes from all other rejection paths.

The earlier issues I flagged were fixed: request-edge policy validation is back, and the replicate docs now match the implementation. I couldn’t re-run the full Go test suite here because the sandbox still blocks `httptest` from binding local ports.

> AGENT

Real finding. The lease markers are intentionally broad to catch various server phrasings, but applying escalation under any run (including `--force-blind` and default best-effort) over-reads "non-fast-forward"/"fetch first" as lease misses when they can be ordinary policy rejections.

Gating the escalation on `s.cfg.ForceWithLease` — the explicit user contract — preserves the lease promise where it was made and leaves BestEffort untouched everywhere else.

> AGENT

Fixed in `5349be76`. `leaseFailureError` now early-returns unless `cfg.ForceWithLease` is set:

- `--force-with-lease` + BestEffort: lease-class rejections still escalate to fatal (the original fix).
- `--force-blind`: broad markers like "non-fast-forward" stay warnable under BestEffort, since blind sends `Old: ZeroHash` and those messages reflect server policy, not a lease miss.
- No force flag + BestEffort: same — markers are ambiguous in this mode and BestEffort's warn-and-continue contract holds.

New test `TestLeaseFailureErrorOnlyAppliesUnderForceWithLease` covers both the non-force and `--force-blind` cases with "non-fast-forward" rejections. Full short test suite green.

{"additions":255,"author":{"id":"MDQ6VXNlcjcwNzQx","is_bot":false,"login":"Soph","name":"Stefan Haubold"},"baseRefName":"main","body":"## Clarify force semantics: `--force-with-lease` and `--force-blind`\r\n\r\nCloses #47.\r\n\r\nThe old `--force` was always lease-protected — it sent the captured session-start target tip as the push command's expected-old, so receive-pack rejected updates where the target had moved during the run. The name oversold the danger: it never matched `git push --force`'s raw clobber.\r\n\r\nReplace it with two explicit flags that mirror git's surface:\r\n\r\n- **`--force-with-lease`** — previous behavior (allow non-FF, captured tip as expected-old; server rejects on lease miss).\r\n- **`--force-blind`** — new path (zero expected-old, overwrite regardless of current target value; matches `git push --force`).\r\n\r\nThe two flags are mutually exclusive. Legacy `--force` errors out with a migration hint. `bootstrap` and `replicate` continue to reject force flags entirely. Pre-0.5, so hard switch — no silent semantics flip.\r\n\r\nOn the API side, `SyncPolicy.Force` splits into `ForceWithLease` + `ForceBlind`. `convert.PlansToPushCommands` takes a `forceBlind` bool; sync's incremental and materialized strategies plumb it through.\r\n\r\nAlso: when receive-pack returns a lease-failure `ng` (stale info / fetch first / non-fast-forward / does not match), the error is wrapped with a \"target ref X moved or differs from session start; rerun, or use --force-blind to overwrite\" hint.\r\n\r\n### Notes\r\n\r\n- Docs: new \"Force Updates and the Per-Run Lease\" section in `docs/usage.md`.\r\n- CHANGELOG left untouched — to be updated at release time.\r\n- Tests cover both convert modes, legacy `--force` rejection, mutual exclusion, replicate-with-force rejection, and the lease-failure annotation.\n\n<!-- CURSOR_SUMMARY -->\n---\n\n> [!NOTE]\n> **Medium Risk**\n> Changes force-push behavior and CLI/API semantics, including a new blind overwrite path that can clobber target refs if misused. Touches core sync planning/push code paths, though guarded by mutual-exclusion checks and expanded tests.\n> \n> **Overview**\n> Replaces the single `--force` knob with two explicit options: **`--force-with-lease`** (previous behavior) and **`--force-blind`** (true overwrite by sending a zero expected-old), enforcing mutual exclusivity and rejecting the legacy `--force` flag with migration guidance.\n> \n> Plumbs the new policy fields through public/unstable API types and internal `syncer` config (`ForceAny()` for callers that only need “force enabled”), and updates strategies to generate push commands accordingly (blind mode zeroes `Old` on non-delete updates).\n> \n> Improves user feedback: planner/block messages now reference `--force-with-lease`, blocked-sync guidance mentions both flags, and receive-pack lease-related `ng` errors are annotated with a rerun/`--force-blind` hint. Docs and tests are updated to cover the new flags and rejection cases (replicate/bootstrap force rejection, legacy flag rejection, and mutual exclusion).\n> \n> <sup>Reviewed by [Cursor Bugbot](https://cursor.com/bugbot) for commit 1ce7b69e386c5edad4543d6e5e489b3b72075757. Configure [here](https://www.cursor.com/dashboard/bugbot).</sup>\n<!-- /CURSOR_SUMMARY -->","changedFiles":21,"comments":[],"deletions":69,"headRefName":"soph/force-clarification","mergeable":"MERGEABLE","reviewRequests":[],"reviews":[{"id":"PRR_kwDOR9SkY87_OtC1","author":{"login":"nodo"},"authorAssociation":"MEMBER","body":"","submittedAt":"2026-05-13T13:24:51Z","includesCreatedEdit":false,"reactionGroups":[],"state":"APPROVED","commit":{"oid":"1ce7b69e386c5edad4543d6e5e489b3b72075757"}}],"statusCheckRollup":[{"__typename":"CheckRun","completedAt":"2026-05-13T13:25:02Z","conclusion":"FAILURE","detailsUrl":"https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/actions/runs/25801957128/job/75794175409","name":"lint","startedAt":"2026-05-13T13:24:32Z","status":"COMPLETED","workflowName":"Lint"},{"__typename":"CheckRun","completedAt":"2026-05-13T13:25:08Z","conclusion":"SUCCESS","detailsUrl":"https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/actions/runs/25801957292/job/75794176278","name":"check-licenses / license-check","startedAt":"2026-05-13T13:24:33Z","status":"COMPLETED","workflowName":"License Check"},{"__typename":"CheckRun","completedAt":"2026-05-13T13:26:00Z","conclusion":"SUCCESS","detailsUrl":"https://github.com/entireio/git-sync/actions/runs/25801957100/job/75794175423","name":"test","startedAt":"2026-05-13T13:24:32Z","status":"COMPLETED","workflowName":"Tests"},{"__typename":"CheckRun","completedAt":"2026-05-13T13:28:30Z","conclusion":"SUCCESS","detailsUrl":"https://cursor.com/docs/bugbot","name":"Cursor Bugbot","startedAt":"2026-05-13T13:24:32Z","status":"COMPLETED","workflowName":""}],"title":"Clearer force params"}
